denote finite-dimensional systems representing the input and the output of the channel, respectively. Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,
\eta\in[0,\sqrt{\varepsilon})
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and let
\psi_{AA^{\prime}}
be a bipartite pure state on the input
A^{\prime}
and on an arbitrary ancillary system
A
. Then, the
n
-shot two-way quantum capacity and the
n
-shot secret-key capacity of
\mathcal{N}
satisfy the following inequalities:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptK^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{N})

\displaystyle\geq Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{N})\geq-H_{\max}^{\sqrt{\varepsilon}-\eta}(A^{n}|B^{n})_{\omega_{AB}^{\otimes n}}+4\log_{2}\eta\,,

(226)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptK^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{N})

\displaystyle\geq Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{N})\geq-H_{\max}^{\sqrt{\varepsilon}-\eta}(B^{n}|A^{n})_{\omega_{AB}^{\otimes n}}+4\log_{2}\eta\,,

(227)

where
\omega_{AB}\coloneqq\mathcal{N}_{A^{\prime}\to B}(\psi_{AA^{\prime}})
, while
A^{n}
and
B^{n}
denote
n
copies of systems
A
and
B
, respectively.

Proof.

The inequality
K^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{N})\geq Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{N})
always holds due to Eq. (
53
). To prove that Eq. (
226
), consider the following
(n,|M|,\varepsilon)
LOCC-assisted quantum communication protocol over
\mathcal{N}_{A^{\prime}\to B}
, where
\log_{2}|M|\geq-H_{\max}^{\sqrt{\varepsilon}-\eta}(A^{n}|B^{n})_{\omega_{AB}^{\otimes n}}+4\log_{2}\eta
:

•

Step (i): Alice sends the half
A^{\prime}

of the state
\psi_{AA^{\prime}}
to Bob via the channel
\mathcal{N}_{A^{\prime}\to B}
for
n
times. This results in them sharing the state
\omega_{AB}^{\otimes n}
.

•

Step (ii): Alice and Bob apply the one-shot entanglement distilation protocol on
\omega_{AB}^{\otimes n}
that achieves the performance stated in Lemma
46
.

Since
Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}
is defined as the maximum
\log_{2}|M|
achievable by any
(n,|M|,\varepsilon)
LOCC-assisted quantum communication protocol over
\mathcal{N}_{A^{\prime}\to B}
, it follows that Eq. (
226
) holds.

To prove (
227
), it suffices to consider a similar
(n,|M|,\varepsilon)
LOCC-assisted quantum communication protocol over
\mathcal{N}_{A^{\prime}\to B}
, where in step (ii), Alice and Bob exchange their roles.
∎

We are now ready to present our lower bounds.

Theorem 48

((Lower bound on the
n
-shot two-way quantum capacity and
n
-shot secret-key capacity of Gaussian channels))
.

Let
\mathcal{N}
be a single-mode Gaussian channel. Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,
\eta\in[0,\sqrt{\varepsilon})
, and
n\in\mathds{N}
such that
n\geq 2\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
. For any pure Gaussian state
\Phi_{AA^{\prime}}
, the
n
-shot two-way quantum capacity and the
n
-shot secret-key capacity of
\mathcal{N}
satisfy the following lower bounds:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptK^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{N})

\displaystyle\geq Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{N})\geq nI_{c}(A\,\rangle\,B)_{\Psi}-\sqrt{n}4\log_{2}({\bar{\eta}_{1}})\sqrt{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2}{\left(\sqrt{\varepsilon}-\eta\right)^{2}}\right)}+4\log_{2}\eta\,,

(228)

\displaystyle K^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{N})

\displaystyle\geq Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{N})\geq nI_{c}(B\,\rangle\,A)_{\Psi}-\sqrt{n}4\log_{2}({\bar{\eta}_{2}})\sqrt{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2}{\left(\sqrt{\varepsilon}-\eta\right)^{2}}\right)}+4\log_{2}\eta\,,

where

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\bar{\eta}_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sqrt{2^{H_{1/2}(A|B)_{\Psi}}}+\sqrt{2^{H_{1/2}(A|E)_{\Psi}}}+1\,,

(229)

\displaystyle\bar{\eta}_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sqrt{2^{H_{1/2}(B|A)_{\Psi}}}+\sqrt{2^{H_{1/2}(B|E)_{\Psi}}}+1\,,

\displaystyle\Psi_{ABE}

\displaystyle\coloneqq V_{A^{\prime}\to BE}\Phi_{AA^{\prime}}(V_{A^{\prime}\to BE})^{\dagger}\,,

and
V_{A^{\prime}\to BE}
is a Stinespring dilation isometry associated with
\mathcal{N}_{A^{\prime}\to B}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptI_{c}(A\,\rangle\,B)_{\Psi}

\displaystyle\coloneqq S(B)_{\Psi}-S(AB)_{\Psi}\,,

(230)

\displaystyle I_{c}(B\,\rangle\,A)_{\Psi}

\displaystyle\coloneqq S(A)_{\Psi}-S(AB)_{\Psi}\,,

\displaystyle H_{1/2}(A|B)_{\Psi}

\displaystyle\coloneqq 2\log_{2}\!\left(\Tr\sqrt{\Psi_{AB}}\,\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes\sqrt{\Psi_{B}}\right)\,,

\displaystyle H_{1/2}(B|A)_{\Psi}

\displaystyle\coloneqq 2\log_{2}\!\left(\Tr\sqrt{\Psi_{AB}}\,\sqrt{\Psi_{A}}\otimes\mathbb{1}_{B}\right)\,,

\displaystyle H_{1/2}(A|E)_{\Psi}

\displaystyle\coloneqq 2\log_{2}\!\left(\Tr\sqrt{\Psi_{AE}}\,\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes\sqrt{\Psi_{E}}\right)\,,

\displaystyle H_{1/2}(B|E)_{\Psi}

\displaystyle\coloneqq 2\log_{2}\!\left(\Tr\sqrt{\Psi_{BE}}\,\mathbb{1}_{B}\otimes\sqrt{\Psi_{E}}\right)\,,

are finite quantities denoting coherent information, reverse coherent information, and conditional Petz Rényi entropies. In particular, by choosing
\eta=\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{2}
, we get

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptK^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{N})

\displaystyle\geq Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{N})\geq nI_{c}(A\,\rangle\,B)_{\Psi}-\sqrt{n}4\log_{2}({\bar{\eta}_{1}})\sqrt{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{8}{\varepsilon}\right)}-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{16}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)\,,

(231)

\displaystyle K^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{N})

\displaystyle\geq Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{N})\geq nI_{c}(B\,\rangle\,A)_{\Psi}-\sqrt{n}4\log_{2}({\bar{\eta}_{2}})\sqrt{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{8}{\varepsilon}\right)}-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{16}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)\,.

In particular, if the input state
\Phi_{AA^{\prime}}
satisfies the energy constraint
\Tr[\hat{N}_{A^{\prime}}\Phi_{AA^{\prime}}]\leq N_{s}
, where
\hat{N}_{A^{\prime}}
is the photon number operator on
A^{\prime}
, then the right-hand sides of (
185
) constitute also a lower bound on the energy-constrained
n
-shot two-way quantum capacity
Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{N},N_{s})
and the energy-constrained
n
-shot secret-key capacity
K^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{N},N_{s})
.

Proof.

The proof exploits Lemma
47
and it is entirely analogous to the proof of Theorem
45
.
∎

C.3
Conditional Petz-Rényi entropy of Gaussian states

The lower bound on the one-shot capacities proved in Theorem
45
and Theorem
48
involves the calculation of the conditional Petz-Rényi entropy of Gaussian states. Therefore, in order to explicitly calculate such bounds for specific examples of Gaussian channels, we need to establish a closed formula for the conditional Petz-Rényi entropy of any Gaussian state in terms of its first moment and covariance matrix. We provide such a closed formula in the forthcoming lemma.

Lemma 49

((Conditional Petz-Rényi entropy with
\alpha=1/2
of Gaussian states))
.

Let
A
be an
m
-mode system and let
B
be an
n
-mode system. Let
\rho_{AB}
be a Gaussian state on
AB
. Then, its conditional Petz-Rényi entropy with
\alpha=1/2
can be calculated as:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptH_{1/2}(A|B)_{\rho_{AB}}=\log_{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\det\!\left(V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\rho_{AB})\right)\,\det\!\left(V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\rho_{B})\right)}}{\det\!\left(\frac{V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\rho_{AB})\big|_{B}+V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\rho_{B})}{2}\right)}\right)\,,

(232)

where we denoted

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptV_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\sigma)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left[\mathbb{1}+\sqrt{\mathbb{1}-(iV(\sigma)\Omega)^{-2}}\right]V(\sigma)\qquad\forall\,\sigma\,,

(233)

and
V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\rho_{AB})\big|_{B}
is the
2n\times 2n
bottom-right block of the matrix
V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\rho_{AB})
.

Before proving the above lemma, let us first understand how to explicitly calculate
V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\sigma)
given
V(\sigma)
.

Remark 1

((Calculation of
V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}
))
.

The definition of
V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\sigma)
includes the term
\sqrt{\mathbb{1}-(iV(\sigma)\Omega)^{-2}}
, which involves applying a function to a (non-Hermitian) matrix. To compute this term, it suffices to diagonalise the matrix
iV(\sigma)\Omega
:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\sqrt{\mathbb{1}-(iV(\sigma)\Omega)^{-2}}=X\sqrt{\mathbb{1}-D^{-2}}X^{-1}\,,

(234)

where
iV(\sigma)\Omega=XDX^{-1}
is the eigendecomposition of
iV(\sigma)\Omega
. The latter can be obtained e.g. using the Williamson’s decomposition of the covariance matrix
V(\sigma)
, where
D
is the diagonal matrix of symplectic eigenvalues satisfying
D\geq\mathbb{1}
. In particular, the latter condition implies that the diagonal matrix
\mathbb{1}-D^{-2}
is real and positive. Consequently, the matrix square root
\sqrt{\mathbb{1}-D^{-2}}
is well-defined.

Proof of Lemma
49
.

The conditional Petz-Rényi entropy with
\alpha=1/2
of
\rho_{AB}
can be expressed in terms of the overlap between the square roots of
\rho_{AB}
and
\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes\rho_{B}
as

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptH_{1/2}(A|B)_{\rho_{AB}}\coloneqq-D_{1/2}(\rho_{AB}\|\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes\rho_{B})=2\log_{2}\!\left(\Tr[\sqrt{\rho_{AB}}\,\sqrt{\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes\rho_{B}}]\right)\,.

(235)

The overlap between two Gaussian states
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
is given by
[
1
]

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Tr[\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}]=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\det\!\left(\frac{V(\sigma_{1})+V(\sigma_{2})}{2}\right)}}e^{-\left(\textbf{m}(\sigma_{1})-\textbf{m}(\sigma_{2})\right)^{\intercal}\left(V(\sigma_{1})+V(\sigma_{2})\right)^{-1}\left(\textbf{m}(\sigma_{1})-\textbf{m}(\sigma_{2})\right)}\,.

(236)

Moreover, the normalised square root
\frac{\sqrt{\sigma}}{\Tr\sqrt{\sigma}}
of a Gaussian state
\sigma
is a Gaussian state with the same first moment as
\sigma
and covariance matrix given by
[
80
, Lemma 2]
(see also
[
81
, Section 3]
and
[
67
]
):

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptV_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\sigma)\coloneqq V\!\left(\frac{\sqrt{\sigma}}{\Tr\sqrt{\sigma}}\right)=\left[\mathbb{1}+\sqrt{\mathbb{1}-(iV(\sigma)\Omega)^{-2}}\right]V(\sigma)\,.

(237)

Consequently, we obtain that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt1=\Tr\!\left[\frac{\sqrt{\sigma}}{\Tr\sqrt{\sigma}}\frac{\sqrt{\sigma}}{\Tr\sqrt{\sigma}}\right]\left(\Tr\sqrt{\sigma}\right)^{2}=\frac{\left(\Tr\sqrt{\sigma}\right)^{2}}{\sqrt{\det\!\left(V\left(\frac{\sqrt{\sigma}}{\Tr\sqrt{\sigma}}\right)\right)}}=\frac{\left(\Tr\sqrt{\sigma}\right)^{2}}{\sqrt{\det\!\left(V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\sigma)\right)}}\,.

(238)

In particular, this implies that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Tr\sqrt{\sigma}=\left[\det\!\left(V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\sigma)\right)\right]^{1/4}\,.

(239)

Hence, we obtain that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Tr[\sqrt{\rho_{AB}}\sqrt{\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes\rho_{B}}]

\displaystyle=\Tr[\sqrt{\rho_{AB}}]\,\Tr[\sqrt{\rho_{B}}]\Tr_{B}\!\left[\frac{\Tr_{A}\!\sqrt{\rho_{AB}}}{\Tr[\sqrt{\rho_{AB}}]}\frac{\sqrt{\rho_{B}}}{\Tr\sqrt{\rho_{B}}}\right]

(240)

\displaystyle=\left[\det\!\left(V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\rho_{AB})\right)\,\det\!\left(V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\rho_{B})\right)\right]^{1/4}\Tr_{B}\!\left[\frac{\Tr_{A}\!\sqrt{\rho_{AB}}}{\Tr[\sqrt{\rho_{AB}}]}\frac{\sqrt{\rho_{B}}}{\Tr\sqrt{\rho_{B}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\left[\det\!\left(V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\rho_{AB})\right)\,\det\!\left(V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\rho_{B})\right)\right]^{1/4}}{\left(\frac{V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\rho_{AB})\big|_{B}+V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\rho_{B})}{2}\right)^{1/2}}\,,

where in the last line we exploited again the formula for the overlap between two Gaussian states and the fact that the two Gaussian states
\frac{\Tr_{A}\!\sqrt{\rho_{AB}}}{\Tr[\sqrt{\rho_{AB}}]}
and
\frac{\sqrt{\rho_{B}}}{\Tr\sqrt{\rho_{B}}}
have the same first moments. Consequently, we conclude that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptH_{1/2}(A|B)_{\rho_{AB}}\coloneqq 2\log_{2}\left(\Tr\left[\sqrt{\rho_{AB}}\sqrt{\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes\rho_{B}}\right]\right)=\log_{2}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\det\!\left(V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\rho_{AB})\right)\,\det\!\left(V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\rho_{B})\right)}}{\det\!\left(\frac{V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\rho_{AB})\big|_{B}+V_{\mathrm{sqrt}}(\rho_{B})}{2}\right)}\right)\,.

(241)

∎

Appendix D
Non-asymptotic analysis of quantum communication across the pure loss channel

This section aims to establish easily computable lower bounds on the
n
-shot quantum capacity, two-way quantum capacity, and secret-key capacity of the pure loss channel
[
1
]
(see Definition
34
for the definition of pure loss channel). This is the most important Gaussian channel as it constitutes the most realistic model to describe optical links, such as optical fibres and free-space links. For each capacity, we will derive two distinct lower bounds: the first is based on the results we obtained in the previous Section
C
exploiting the asymptotic equipartition property; the second is based on the analysis of the
entropy variance

[
47
]
presented in the forthcoming Subsection
D.2
.

D.1
Asymptotic equipartition property approach

The goal of this section is to evaluate the lower bounds on the
n
-shot capacities proved in Theorem
45
and Theorem
48
for the physically interesting case of the pure loss channel
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
.

Let us apply such theorems by choosing as input state
\Phi_{AA^{\prime}}
the two-mode squeezed vacuum state
\ket{\Psi_{N_{s}}}\!\!\bra{\Psi_{N_{s}}}_{AA^{\prime}}
with local mean photon number equal to
N_{s}
, as defined in Eq. (
94
). Moreover, let us recall that the pure loss channel
\pazocal{E}_{\lambda}
, defined in Definition
34
, is a single-mode Gaussian channel that admits the following Stinespring dilation:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\pazocal{E}_{\lambda}(\cdot)=\Tr_{E}\left[U_{\lambda}^{A^{\prime}E\to BE}\big((\cdot)\otimes\ket{0}\!\!\bra{0}_{E}\big)(U_{\lambda}^{A^{\prime}E\to BE})^{\dagger}\right]\,,

(242)

where
U_{\lambda}^{A^{\prime}E\to BE}
denotes the beam splitter unitary defined in Definition
30
. In order to apply Theorem
45
and Theorem
48
, we need to calculate the covariance matrix
V\!\left(\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}\right)
of the tri-partite state

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}\coloneqq\left(\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes U_{\lambda}^{BE}\right)\,\left(\ket{\Psi_{N_{s}}}\!\!\bra{\Psi_{N_{s}}}_{AB}\otimes\ket{0}\!\!\bra{0}_{E}\right)\left(\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes U_{\lambda}^{BE}\right)^{\dagger}\,,

(243)

e.g. with respect the mode-ordering
(A,B,E)
. By exploiting Eq. (
99
) and Eq. (
103
), it follows that
V\!\left(\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}\right)
can be expressed as

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptV\!\left(\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}\right)=\left(\mathbb{1}_{2}\oplus{S}_{\lambda}\right)\left(V(\ket{\Psi_{N_{s}}}\!\!\bra{\Psi_{N_{s}}}_{AB})\oplus V(\ket{0}\!\!\bra{0})\right)\left(\mathbb{1}_{2}\oplus{S}_{\lambda}^{\intercal}\right)\,,

(244)

where:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptS_{\lambda}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(\begin{matrix}\sqrt{\lambda}\,\mathbb{1}_{2}&\sqrt{1-\lambda}\,\mathbb{1}_{2}\\
-\sqrt{1-\lambda}\,\mathbb{1}_{2}&\sqrt{\lambda}\,\mathbb{1}_{2}\end{matrix}\right)\,,

(245)

is the symplectic matrix associated with the beam splitter unitary,
V(\ket{\Psi_{N_{s}}}\!\!\bra{\Psi_{N_{s}}})
is the covariance matrix of the two-mode squeezed vacuum state given in Eq. (
95
), and
V(\ket{0}\!\!\bra{0})=\mathbb{1}_{2}
is the covariance matrix of the vacuum state. By explicitly performing the matrix multiplications, one obtains that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt{V\!\left(\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}\right)=\begin{bmatrix}1+2N_{s}&0&2\sqrt{\lambda N_{s}(1+N_{s})}&0&-2\sqrt{(1-\lambda)N_{s}(1+N_{s})}&0\\
0&1+2N_{s}&0&-2\sqrt{\lambda N_{s}(1+N_{s})}&0&2\sqrt{(1-\lambda)N_{s}(1+N_{s})}\\
2\sqrt{\lambda N_{s}(1+N_{s})}&0&1+2\lambda N_{s}&0&-2\sqrt{(1-\lambda)\lambda}N_{s}&0\\
0&-2\sqrt{\lambda N_{s}(1+N_{s})}&0&1+2\lambda N_{s}&0&-2\sqrt{(1-\lambda)\lambda}N_{s}\\
-2\sqrt{(1-\lambda)N_{s}(1+N_{s})}&0&-2\sqrt{(1-\lambda)\lambda}N_{s}&0&1+2(1-\lambda)N_{s}&0\\
0&2\sqrt{(1-\lambda)N_{s}(1+N_{s})}&0&-2\sqrt{(1-\lambda)\lambda}N_{s}&0&1+2(1-\lambda)N_{s}\\
\end{bmatrix}\,.}

(246)

Hence, by exploiting Lemma
49
, one can explicitly calculate all the conditional Petz-Rényi entropies involved in Theorem
45
and Theorem
48
. By a simple calculation performed on the Mathematica notebook attached to this manuscript, one obtains:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptH_{1/2}(A|B)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\left(\frac{\left[1+2(1-\lambda)N_{s}+2\sqrt{(1-\lambda)N_{s}[1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}]}\right]\left[1+2\lambda N_{s}+2\sqrt{\lambda N_{s}(1+\lambda N_{s})}\right]}{\left[1+\sqrt{\lambda N_{s}(1+\lambda N_{s})}+\lambda\left(2N_{s}+\sqrt{\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}^{3}}{1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}}}\right)\right]^{2}}\right)\,,

(247)

\displaystyle H_{1/2}(A|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\left(\frac{\left[1+2(1-\lambda)N_{s}+2\sqrt{(1-\lambda)N_{s}(1+(1-\lambda)N_{s})}\right]\left[1+2\lambda N_{s}+2\sqrt{\lambda N_{s}(1+\lambda N_{s})}\right]}{\left[1+\sqrt{(1-\lambda)N_{s}(1+(1-\lambda)N_{s})}+(1-\lambda)\left(2N_{s}+\sqrt{\frac{\lambda N_{s}^{3}}{1+\lambda N_{s}}}\right)\right]^{2}}\right)\,,

\displaystyle H_{1/2}(B|A)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\left(\frac{\left[1+2N_{s}+2\sqrt{N_{s}(1+N_{s})}\right]\left[1+2(1-\lambda)N_{s}+2\sqrt{(1-\lambda)N_{s}(1+(1-\lambda)N_{s})}\right]}{\left[1+2N_{s}+\sqrt{N_{s}(1+N_{s})}+(1+N_{s})\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}}}\right]^{2}}\right)\,,

\displaystyle H_{1/2}(B|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\left(\frac{\left(1+2(1-\lambda)N_{s}+2\sqrt{(1-\lambda)N_{s}(1+(1-\lambda)N_{s})}\right)(1+2N_{s}+2\sqrt{N_{s}(1+N_{s})})}{\left[1+(1-\lambda)\left(2N_{s}+\sqrt{N_{s}(1+N_{s})}\right)+\sqrt{(1-\lambda)N_{s}(1+(1-\lambda)N_{s})}\right]^{2}}\right)\,.

Moreover, thanks to Eq. (
121
) and Eq. (
123
), we can also obtain the coherent information quantities appearing in Theorem
45
and Theorem
48
:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptI_{c}(A\,\rangle\,B)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=h(\lambda N_{s})-h\!\left((1-\lambda)N_{s}\right)\,,

(248)

\displaystyle I_{c}(B\,\rangle\,A)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=h(N_{s})-h\!\left((1-\lambda)N_{s}\right)\,,

where
h(x)\coloneqq(x+1)\log_{2}(x+1)-x\log_{2}x
. By taking the limit of infinite energy
N_{s}\rightarrow\infty
, one obtains

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\lim\limits_{N_{s}\rightarrow\infty}H_{1/2}(A|B)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{1-\lambda}{\lambda}\right)\,,

(249)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{N_{s}\rightarrow\infty}H_{1/2}(A|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{1-\lambda}{\lambda}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{N_{s}\rightarrow\infty}H_{1/2}(B|A)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\!\left(1-\lambda\right)\,,

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{N_{s}\rightarrow\infty}H_{1/2}(B|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=-\log_{2}\!\left(1-\lambda\right)\,.

and, thanks to Eq. (
117
) and Eq. (
118
), one also obtains that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\lim\limits_{N_{s}\rightarrow\infty}I_{c}(A\,\rangle\,B)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{\lambda}{1-\lambda}\right)\,,

(250)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{N_{s}\rightarrow\infty}I_{c}(B\,\rangle\,A)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\lambda}\right)\,.

By putting together Eq. (
249
), Eq. (
250
), and Theorem
45
, we can obtain a lower bound on the
n
-shot quantum capacity of the pure loss channel.

Theorem 50

((Lower bound on the
n
-shot quantum capacity of the pure loss channel))
.

Let
\lambda\in(0,1)
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, and
n\in\mathds{N}
such that
n\geq 2\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
. The
n
-shot quantum capacity of the pure loss channel satisfies:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptQ^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq nQ(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})-\sqrt{n}4\log_{2}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{1-\lambda}{\lambda}}+\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{1-\lambda}}+1\right)\sqrt{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{9}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)}-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{18}}{3\varepsilon^{4}}\right)\,,

(251)

where

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptQ(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{\lambda}{1-\lambda}\right)&\text{if $\lambda\in(\frac{1}{2},1]$ ,}\\
0&\text{if $\lambda\in[0,\frac{1}{2}]$ ,}\end{cases}

(252)

is the (asymptotic) quantum capacity of the pure loss channel.

As a consequence of the above theorem,
n
uses of the pure loss channel
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
allows one to transmit a number of qubits equal to the right hand of Eq. (
251
) with an error not exceeding
\varepsilon
. Here, the error is measured as in Eq. (
37
) in terms of the channel fidelity.

By combining Eq. (
249
), Eq. (
250
), and Theorem
48
, we can obtain a lower bound on the
n
-shot two-way quantum capacity and
n
-shot secret-key capacity of the pure loss channel.

Theorem 51

((Lower bound on the
n
-shot two-way quantum capacity and
n
-shot secret-key capacity of the pure loss channel))
.

Let
\lambda\in(0,1)
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, and
n\in\mathds{N}
such that
n\geq 2\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
. The
n
-shot two-way quantum capacity and the
n
-shot secret-key capacity of the pure loss channel satisfy:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptK^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})\geq Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq nQ_{2}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})-\sqrt{n}4\log_{2}\!\left(\sqrt{1-\lambda}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{1-\lambda}}+1\right)\sqrt{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{8}{\varepsilon}\right)}-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{16}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)\,,

(253)

where
Q_{2}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})\coloneqq\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\lambda}\right)
is the (asymptotic) two-way quantum capacity of the pure loss channel.

As a result of the above theorem,
n
uses of the pure loss channel
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
and arbitrary LOCCs allow one to generate a number of ebits and a number of secret-key bits equal to the right hand of Eq. (
253
) with an error not exceeding
\varepsilon
.

By using (
247
), (
248
),Theorem
45
, and Theorem
48
, we can also find a lower bound on the energy-constrained
n
-shot capacities of the pure loss channel, as stated in the following theorem.

Theorem 52

((Lower bound on the energy-constrained
n
-shot capacities of the pure loss channel))
.

Let
N_{s}>0
,
\lambda\in(0,1)
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, and
n\in\mathds{N}
such that
n\geq 2\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
. The energy-constrained
n
-shot quantum capacity of the pure loss channel satisfies:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptQ^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},N_{s})\geq n\left(h(\lambda N_{s})-h\!\left((1-\lambda)N_{s}\right)\right)-\sqrt{n}4\log_{2}\left(\sqrt{2^{H_{1/2}(A|B)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}}}+\sqrt{2^{H_{1/2}(A|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}}}+1\right)\sqrt{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{9}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)}-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{18}}{3\varepsilon^{4}}\right)\,,

(254)

where
H_{1/2}(A|B)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}
and
H_{1/2}(A|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}
are reported in (
247
). Moreover, the energy-constrained
n
-shot two-way quantum and secret-key capacity of the pure loss channel satisfy:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptK^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},N_{s})

\displaystyle\geq Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},N_{s})

(255)

\displaystyle\geq n\left(h(N_{s})-h\!\left((1-\lambda)N_{s}\right)\right)-\sqrt{n}4\log_{2}\left(\sqrt{2^{H_{1/2}(B|A)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}}}+\sqrt{2^{H_{1/2}(B|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}}}+1\right)\sqrt{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{8}{\varepsilon}\right)}-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{16}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)\,,

where
H_{1/2}(B|A)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}
and
H_{1/2}(B|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}
are reported in (
247
).

D.2
Entropy variance approach

In the previous section we found a lower bound on the
n
-shot capacities by bounding the smooth max relative entropy (see Definition
24
) via the infinite-dimensional asymptotic equipartition property (see Lemma
43
). An alternative approach to lower bound the
n
-shot capacities can be obtained from a different bound on the smooth max relative entropy in terms of the so-called
relative entropy variance
.

Definition 53

((Relative entropy variance and Conditional entropy variance))
.

For finite-dimensional Hilbert spaces, the relative entropy variance between a state
\rho
and a positive semi-definite operator
\sigma
(in finite dimension) is defined by

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptV(\rho\|\sigma)\coloneqq\Tr[\rho(\log_{2}\rho-\log_{2}\sigma)^{2}]-D(\rho\|\sigma)^{2}\,.

(256)

For separable Hilbert spaces, this expression has to be meant as a converging series. The conditional entropy variance of a bipartite state
\rho
on the joint system
AB
is defined as

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptV(A|B)_{\rho}\coloneqq V(\rho_{AB}\|\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes\rho_{B})\,.

(257)

The forthcoming Lemma
54
, presented in
[
48
]
(previously stated in the finite-dimensional setting in
[
82
]
and later generalised to von Neumann algebras in
[
63
]
), establishes a connection between the smooth max-relative entropy
D_{\max}^{\varepsilon}(\rho\|\sigma)
and the
\varepsilon
-hypothesis testing relative entropy

D^{\varepsilon}_{h}(\rho\|\sigma)
, defined by

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptD^{\varepsilon}_{h}(\rho\|\sigma)\coloneqq-\log_{2}\inf_{E}\left\{\Tr[E\sigma]\,:\,0\leq E\leq\mathbb{1},\,\Tr[E\rho]\geq 1-\varepsilon\right\}\,.

(258)

Lemma 54
.

Let
\rho
be a state and
\sigma
a positive semi-definite operator on a separable Hilbert space. Let
\varepsilon,\delta>0
such that
\varepsilon+\delta<1
and
D_{\max}^{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}(\rho\|\sigma)<\infty
. Then, it holds that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptD_{\max}^{\varepsilon}(\rho\|\sigma)

\displaystyle\leq D^{1-\varepsilon}_{h}(\rho\|\sigma)+\log_{2}\left(\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}\right)\,,

(259)

\displaystyle D^{1-\delta-\varepsilon}_{h}(\rho\|\sigma)

\displaystyle\leq D^{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}_{\max}(\rho\|\sigma)+\log_{2}\left(\frac{4(1-\delta)}{1-\varepsilon}\right)\,.

The following is an immediate consequence of
[
29
, Proposition 31]
.

Lemma 55

((Upper bound for the hypothesis testing relative entropy))
.

Let
\rho
be a positive definite state and
\sigma
be a positive definite operator with bounded trace on a separable Hilbert space. For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
we have that

\displaystyle D^{\varepsilon}_{h}(\rho^{\otimes n}\|\sigma^{\otimes n})

\displaystyle\leq nD(\rho\|\sigma)+\sqrt{\frac{{nV(\rho\|\sigma)}}{1-\varepsilon}}+\log_{2}6+2\log_{2}\frac{1+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}.

(260)

Note that
[
29
, Proposition 31]
is originally stated in the case in which
\sigma
is a state. However, it can be readily extended to positive semi-definite operators by considering the definitions of
D^{\varepsilon}_{h}(\rho\|\sigma)
,
D(\rho\|\sigma)
, and
V(\rho\|\sigma)
. Additionally, note that the original statement employs the definitions of these quantities in terms of natural logarithms, but we have adjusted it to align with our convention of base-two logarithms.

As a consequence of Lemma (
54
) and Lemma (
55
), we have the following.

Lemma 56
.

Let
\rho
be a positive definite state and
\sigma
be a positive definite operator with bounded trace on a separable Hilbert space. For any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
we have that

\displaystyle D_{\max}^{\varepsilon}(\rho^{\otimes n}\|\sigma^{\otimes n})\leq nD(\rho\|\sigma)+\sqrt{\frac{{nV(\rho\|\sigma)}}{\varepsilon}}+\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{6(2-\varepsilon)^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}(1-\varepsilon)}\right)\,.

(261)

Let us state the following lower bound on the
n
-shot quantum capacity of the pure loss channel.

Lemma 57
.

Let
\lambda\in(0,1)
and
N_{s}>0
. For all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the
n
-shot quantum capacity of
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
can be lower bounded in terms of the quantum relative entropy and the conditional entropy variance as follows:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptQ^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})\geq-nD(\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}\|\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes\Psi^{(k)}_{E})-4\sqrt{\frac{{nV(A|E)_{\Psi^{(k)}}}}{\varepsilon}}-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{23}(32-\varepsilon)^{2}}{(16-\varepsilon)\varepsilon^{6}}\right)\,,

(262)

where
\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}
is the reduced state on systems
A,E
of the tri-partite state
\Psi_{ABE}^{(k)}\coloneqq\ket{\Psi^{(k)}}\!\!\bra{\Psi^{(k)}}_{ABE}
on systems
A,B,E
defined as

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\ket{\Psi^{(k)}}_{ABE}\coloneqq\frac{P_{k}^{A}\ket{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}_{ABE}}{\sqrt{\Tr[P_{k}\tau_{N_{s}}]}}\,.

(263)

Here,
\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}_{ABE}\coloneqq\ket{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}\!\!\bra{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}_{ABE}
is a tri-partite state on systems
A,B,E
defined as

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\ket{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}_{ABE}\coloneqq\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes U_{\lambda}^{BE}\,\ket{\psi_{N_{s}}}_{AB}\otimes\ket{0}_{E}\,;

(264)

U_{\lambda}^{BE}
is the beam splitter unitary defined in Definition
30
;
\ket{\psi_{N_{s}}}_{AB}
is the two-mode squeezed vacuum state defined in Eq. (
94
);
\tau^{A}_{N_{s}}\coloneqq\Tr_{B}\ket{\psi_{N_{s}}}\!\!\bra{\psi_{N_{s}}}_{AB}
is the thermal state with mean photon number
N_{s}
;
\ket{0}_{E}
denoted the vacuum state on
E
; and
P_{k}^{A}\coloneqq\sum_{n=0}^{k}\ket{n}\!\!\bra{n}_{A}
is the projector on the first
k+1
Fock states of
A
.

In addition, the right-hand side of (
262
) constitutes also a lower bound on the energy-constrained
n
-shot quantum capacity
Q^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},N_{s})
.

Proof.

Let us employ same notation used in the proof of Theorem
45
, where now the input state is chosen to be the two-mode squeezed vacuum state
\ket{\psi_{N_{s}}}_{AB}
. By exploiting (
197
), we have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptQ^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq-D_{\max}^{\frac{\varepsilon\sqrt{\delta}}{4}-\eta}\left((\Psi^{(k)}_{A_{k}EE^{\prime}})^{\otimes n}\|\mathbb{1}_{A_{k}^{n}}\otimes(\Psi^{(k)}_{EE^{\prime}})^{\otimes n}\right)+\log_{2}\!\left(\eta^{4}(1-\delta)\right)\,,

(265)

where, thanks to (
200
), the state
\Psi^{(k)}_{A_{k}B_{k}EE^{\prime}}
can be written as

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\ket{\Psi_{A_{k}B_{k}EE^{\prime}}^{(k)}}=V_{A\to A_{k}}\otimes V_{B\to B_{k}}\otimes\mathbb{1}_{EE^{\prime}}\frac{P_{k}^{A}\otimes W_{B\to BE^{\prime}}^{(k)}\ket{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}_{ABE}}}{\sqrt{\Tr[P_{k}\tau_{N_{s}}]}}\,.

(266)

We have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptP_{k}^{A}\otimes\mathbb{1}\ket{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}_{ABE}}=\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes U_{\lambda}^{BE}\,(P_{k}^{A}\otimes\mathbb{1}_{B}\ket{\psi_{N_{s}}}_{AB})\otimes\ket{0}_{E}\,.

(267)

and that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptP_{k}^{A}\otimes\mathbb{1}_{B}\ket{\psi_{N_{s}}}_{AB}=P_{k}^{A}\otimes P_{k}^{B}\ket{\psi_{N_{s}}}_{AB}\,,

(268)

where the last equality follows from the definition of two-mode squeezed vacuum state in Eq. (
94
).
Now, we apply the fact that the beam splitter unitary can not increase the total number of photons. Specifically, from Eq. (
106
) it easily follows that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes U_{\lambda}^{BE}\,(P_{k}^{A}\otimes P_{k}^{B}\otimes\mathbb{1}_{E})\ket{\psi_{N_{s}}}_{AB}\otimes\ket{0}_{E}

\displaystyle=(P_{k}^{A}\otimes P_{k}^{B}\otimes\mathbb{1}_{E})\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes U_{\lambda}^{BE}\ket{\psi_{N_{s}}}_{AB}\otimes\ket{0}_{E}\,,

(269)

i.e.

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptP_{k}^{A}\otimes\mathbb{1}\ket{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}_{ABE}}=(P_{k}^{A}\otimes P_{k}^{B}\otimes\mathbb{1}_{E})\ket{\psi_{N_{s}}^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}_{ABE}\,.

(270)

Consequently, from the definition of
W_{B\to BE^{\prime}}^{(k)}
in (
194
) it follows that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptP_{k}^{A}\otimes W_{B\to BE^{\prime}}^{(k)}\ket{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}_{ABE}}=P_{k}^{A}\otimes\mathbb{1}_{BEE^{\prime}}\ket{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}_{ABE}}\otimes\ket{0}_{E^{\prime}}\,.

(271)

and hence (
266
) implies that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\ket{\Psi_{A_{k}B_{k}EE^{\prime}}^{(k)}}=V_{A\to A_{k}}\otimes V_{B\to B_{k}}\otimes\mathbb{1}_{EE^{\prime}}\left(\frac{P_{k}^{A}\otimes\mathbb{1}_{BE}\ket{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}_{ABE}}}{\sqrt{\Tr[P_{k}\tau_{N_{s}}]}}\right)\otimes\ket{0}_{E^{\prime}}\,.

(272)

We are now ready to apply Lemma
56
. Note that one of the hypotheses of Lemma
56
is satisfied because the operator
\sigma\coloneqq\mathbb{1}_{A_{k}}\otimes\Psi^{(k)}_{EE^{\prime}}
has bounded trace (its trace equals
k+1
). The hypothesis that
\sigma
is positive definite is not satisfied because here
\sigma
is just positive semi-definite (and not positive definite). However, one can employ a completely analogous reasoning as in
[
29
, Theorem 24]
to make the the statement of Lemma
56
valid also in this case. Indeed, by following the reasoning of
[
29
, Theorem 24]
, we can use that
D^{\varepsilon}_{\max}(\rho\|\sigma)\leq D^{\varepsilon+\frac{1}{2}\|\rho-\rho^{\prime}\|_{1}}_{\max}(\rho^{\prime}\|\sigma)
, which is valid for any state
\rho^{\prime}
, and choose

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\rho^{\prime}=\rho_{k,N_{\mathrm{add}}}\coloneqq\frac{P^{E}_{k}P^{E^{\prime}}_{k}\mathcal{E}_{N_{\mathrm{add}},EE^{\prime}}((\Psi^{(k)}_{A_{k}EE^{\prime}})^{\otimes n})P^{E}_{k}P^{E^{\prime}}_{k}}{\Tr[P_{k}^{E}P^{E^{\prime}}_{k}\mathcal{E}_{N_{\mathrm{add},EE^{\prime}}}((\Psi^{(k)}_{A_{k}EE^{\prime}})^{\otimes n})]}\,,

(273)

where
\mathcal{E}_{N_{\mathrm{add}},EE^{\prime}}
is an additive noise channel with parameter
N_{\mathrm{add}}
acting on system
EE^{\prime}
(such that
N_{\mathrm{add}}=0
corresponds to the identity channel), so that
\lim_{N_{\mathrm{add}}\rightarrow 0}\|\rho-\rho^{\prime}\|_{1}=0
. In the achievability bounds we are allowed to choose any admissible
\sigma
, see Definition
25
and how this is applied in (
197
); thus we can take it to be
\sigma_{k,N_{\mathrm{add}}}\coloneqq\mathbb{1}_{A_{k}}\otimes\Tr_{A_{k}}[\rho_{k,N_{\mathrm{add}}}]
. In this way
\sigma
is full rank and Lemma
56
can be applied. In the end, we can send
N_{\mathrm{add}}
to zero, and we are left to check that the limits of the conditional entropy and of the variance of the conditional entropy converge to their value when
N_{\mathrm{add}}=0
. One can be convinced of this fact by noticing that to evaluate these expression on the considered class of states one only needs the diagonal density matrix elements in the Fock basis (since
\sigma_{k,N_{\mathrm{add}}}
is diagonal in this basis), which are finitely many due to the projections
P_{k}^{E}
and
P_{k}^{E^{\prime}}
, and converge to non-zero numbers corresponding to the case
N_{\mathrm{add}}=0
. Hence, we conclude that the statement of Lemma
56
is valid also when
\sigma
is just positive semi-definite without being positive definite.

Consequently, by exploiting Lemma
56
and Eq. (
265
) with
\delta=\frac{1}{4}
and
\eta=\frac{\varepsilon}{16}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptQ^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq-nD(\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}\|\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes\Psi^{(k)}_{E})-4\sqrt{\frac{{nV(A|E)_{\Psi^{(k)}}}}{\varepsilon}}-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{23}(32-\varepsilon)^{2}}{(16-\varepsilon)\varepsilon^{6}}\right)\,.

(274)

Since the mean photon number on
B
of the two-mode squeezed vacuum state
\ket{\psi_{N_{s}}}_{AB}
is equal to
N_{s}
, it follows that the right-hand side of Eq. (
274
) constitutes also a lower bound on the energy-constrained
n
-shot quantum capacity
Q^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},N_{s})
.
∎

Similarly, we obtain a lower bound on the
n
-shot two-way quantum capacity and secret-key capacity of the pure loss channel.

Lemma 58
.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. By employing the same notation as in Lemma
57
, the
n
-shot two-way quantum capacity and the
n
-shot secret-key capacity of the pure loss channel
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
can be lower bounded in terms of the quantum relative entropy and the conditional entropy variance as follows:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptK^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})\geq Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq-nD(\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}\|\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes\Psi^{(k)}_{E})-\sqrt{\frac{{2nV(A|E)_{\Psi^{(k)}}}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}}-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{6}\,3\,(4-\sqrt{\varepsilon})^{2}}{(2-\sqrt{\varepsilon})\varepsilon^{3}}\right)\,,

(275)

\displaystyle K^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})\geq Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq-nD(\Psi^{(k)}_{BE}\|\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes\Psi^{(k)}_{E})-\sqrt{\frac{{2nV(B|E)_{\Psi^{(k)}}}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}}-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{6}\,3\,(4-\sqrt{\varepsilon})^{2}}{(2-\sqrt{\varepsilon})\varepsilon^{3}}\right)\,.

In addition, the right-hand sides of (
275
) constitute also a lower bound on the energy-constrained
n
-shot two-way quantum capacity
Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},N_{s})
and energy-constrained
n
-shot two-way secret-key capacity
K^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},N_{s})
.

Proof.

The proof is entirely analogous to the proof of Lemma
57
. In particular, it exploits Lemma
47
with
\eta=\frac{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}{2}
and Lemma
56
.
∎

In the following, we will compute
V(B|E)_{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}
and
V(A|E)_{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}
and show that
V(B|E)_{\Psi^{(k)}}
and
V(A|E)_{\Psi^{(k)}}
converge to them. We have an explicit proof for the states at hand; it is possible that a more general statement can be made for states which have bounded second moments, but we leave it for future work.

Let us start by stating the following lemma.

Lemma 59
.

Let
\lambda\in(0,1),N_{s}>0
, and

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\ket{\Psi}_{ABE}\coloneqq\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes U_{\lambda}^{BE}\,\ket{\psi_{N_{s}}}_{AB}\otimes\ket{0}_{E}\,,

(276)

where
U_{\lambda}^{BE}
is the beam splitter unitary defined in Definition
30
;
\ket{\psi_{N_{s}}}_{AB}
is the two-mode squeezed vacuum state defined in Eq. (
94
); and
\ket{0}_{E}
is the vacuum state on
E
. Then, it holds that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\ket{\Psi}_{ABE}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{s}+1}}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{n/2}(-1)^{l}\sqrt{\binom{n}{l}\lambda^{n-l}(1-\lambda)^{l}}\ket{n}_{A}\otimes\ket{n-l}_{B}\otimes\ket{l}_{E}\,.

(277)

In particular, it holds that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Psi_{A}

\displaystyle=\tau_{N_{s}}\,,

(278)

\displaystyle\Psi_{B}

\displaystyle=\tau_{\lambda N_{s}}\,,

\displaystyle\Psi_{E}

\displaystyle=\tau_{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}\,,

Proof.

(
277
) easily follows by exploiting the definition of two-mode squeezed vacuum state and by employing (
106
). Moreover, (
278
) follows by taking the partial traces of (
277
) and by exploiting the fact
\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}N\!B(n;r,p)=1
, where

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptN\!B(n;r,p)\coloneqq\binom{n+r-1}{n}(1-p)^{n}p^{r}\quad\forall\,p\in[0,1],n\in\mathbb{N},r\in\mathbb{N}\,,

(279)

is the
negative binomial distribution
.
∎

The following lemma allows us to compute
V(B|E)_{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}
and
V(A|E)_{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}
.

Lemma 60
.

Let
\lambda\in(0,1),N_{s}>0
, and

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\ket{\Psi}_{ABE}\coloneqq\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes U_{\lambda}^{BE}\,\ket{\psi_{N_{s}}}_{AB}\otimes\ket{0}_{E}\,,

(280)

be the state defined in (
276
). Then, by denoting
\Psi_{ABE}\coloneqq\ket{\Psi}\!\!\bra{\Psi}_{ABE}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptV(A|E)_{\Psi}=V(A|B)_{\Psi}

\displaystyle=\lambda N_{s}(1+\lambda N_{s})\left(\log_{2}\frac{\lambda N_{s}}{1+\lambda N_{s}}\right)^{2}

(281)

\displaystyle+(1-\lambda)N_{s}(1+(1-\lambda)N_{s})\left(\log_{2}\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-2(1-\lambda)\lambda N_{s}^{2}\log_{2}\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}}\log_{2}\frac{\lambda N_{s}}{1+\lambda N_{s}}\,,

\displaystyle V(B|E)_{\Psi}=V(B|A)_{\Psi}

\displaystyle=N_{s}(1+N_{s})\left(\log_{2}\frac{N_{s}}{1+N_{s}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+(1-\lambda)N_{s}(1+(1-\lambda)N_{s})\left(\log_{2}\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-2(1-\lambda)N_{s}(1+N_{s})\left(\log_{2}\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}}\right)\left(\log_{2}\frac{N_{s}}{1+N_{s}}\right),

Consequently, the following limits hold:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\lim_{N_{s}\rightarrow\infty}V(A|E)_{\Psi}

\displaystyle=\lim_{N_{s}\rightarrow\infty}V(A|B)_{\Psi}=0\,,

(282)

\displaystyle\lim_{N_{s}\rightarrow\infty}V(B|E)_{\Psi}

\displaystyle=\lim_{N_{s}\rightarrow\infty}V(B|A)_{\Psi}=0\,.

Proof.

The expression of
V(B|E)_{\Psi_{AB}}
in (
281
) has already been proved in
[
47
]
. Let us sketch the proof presented in
[
47
]
, as it will be useful for the rest of our proof. As proved in
[
47
]
, from the definition of conditional entropy variance one can easily see that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptV(B|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}}

\displaystyle=\Tr[\Psi_{A}(\log_{2}\Psi_{A})^{2}]-\Tr[\Psi_{A}\log_{2}\Psi_{A}]^{2}

(283)

\displaystyle+\Tr[\Psi_{E}(\log_{2}\Psi_{E})^{2}]-\Tr[\Psi_{E}\log_{2}\Psi_{E}]^{2}

\displaystyle-2\Tr[\Psi_{AE}(\log_{2}\Psi_{A})(\log_{2}\Psi_{E})]+2\Tr[\Psi_{E}\log_{2}\Psi_{E}]\Tr[\Psi_{A}\log_{2}\Psi_{A}]\,.

Since this expression is symmetric under exchanging
E
and
A
, it follows that
V(B|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}}=V(B|A)_{\Psi_{ABE}}
. Additionally, Lemma
59
establishes that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Psi_{A}

\displaystyle=\tau_{N_{s}}\,,

(284)

\displaystyle\Psi_{E}

\displaystyle=\tau_{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}\,.

Hence, thanks to (
96
), we have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-\Tr[\Psi_{A}\log_{2}\Psi_{A}]

\displaystyle=h(N_{s})\,,

(285)

\displaystyle-\Tr[\Psi_{E}\log_{2}\Psi_{E}]

\displaystyle=h\!\left((1-\lambda)N_{s}\right)\,,

where
h(x)\coloneqq(x+1)\log_{2}(x+1)-x\log_{2}x
. Furthermore, the first two terms in
283
are the entropy variances of
\Psi_{A}
and
\Psi_{E}
, which are both thermal states. Using (
97
), these terms can be expressed as

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Tr[\Psi_{A}(\log_{2}\Psi_{A})^{2}]-\Tr[\Psi_{A}\log_{2}\Psi_{A}]^{2}

\displaystyle=N_{s}(N_{s}+1)\log_{2}\left(1+\frac{1}{N_{s}}\right)^{2}\,,

(286)

\displaystyle\Tr[\Psi_{E}(\log_{2}\Psi_{E})^{2}]-\Tr[\Psi_{E}\log_{2}\Psi_{E}]^{2}

\displaystyle=(1-\lambda)N_{s}\left[(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1\right]\log_{2}\left(1+\frac{1}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}\right)^{2}\,.

Hence, we have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptV(B|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}}

\displaystyle=N_{s}(N_{s}+1)\log_{2}\left(1+\frac{1}{N_{s}}\right)^{2}

(287)

\displaystyle+(1-\lambda)N_{s}\left[(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1\right]\log_{2}\left(1+\frac{1}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-2\left[\Tr[\Psi_{AE}(\log_{2}\Psi_{A})(\log_{2}\Psi_{E})]-h(N_{s})h((1-\lambda)N_{s})\right]

Hence, to complete the calculation of
V(B|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}}
, we only need to calculate the term
\Tr[\Psi_{AE}(\log_{2}\Psi_{A})(\log_{2}\Psi_{E})]
. To this end, note that the operator
\log_{2}\tau_{N_{s}}
can be written in terms of the photon number operator
\hat{N}_{n}
as

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\log_{2}\tau_{N_{s}}=\log_{2}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)\hat{N}-\log_{2}(1+N_{s})\mathbb{1}\,.

(288)

Consequently, by denoting as
\hat{N}_{A}
and
\hat{N}_{E}
the photon number operators on
A
and
E
, we have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\log_{2}\Psi_{A}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)\hat{N}_{A}-\log_{2}(1+N_{s})\mathbb{1}_{A}\,,

(289)

\displaystyle\log_{2}\Psi_{E}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\left(\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1}\right)\hat{N}_{E}-\log_{2}(1+(1-\lambda)N_{s})\mathbb{1}_{E}\,,

and thus

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Tr[\Psi_{AE}(\log_{2}\Psi_{A})(\log_{2}\Psi_{E})]

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)\log_{2}\left(\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1}\right)\Tr[\Psi_{AE}\hat{N}_{A}\otimes\hat{N}_{E}]

(290)

\displaystyle-\log_{2}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)\log_{2}\left(1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}\right)\Tr[\Psi_{A}\hat{N}_{A}]

\displaystyle-\log_{2}\left(\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1}\right)\log_{2}\left(1+N_{s}\right)\Tr[\Psi_{E}\hat{N}_{E}]

\displaystyle+\log_{2}\left(1+N_{s}\right)\log_{2}\left(1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}\right)

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)\log_{2}\left(\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1}\right)\Tr[\Psi_{AE}\hat{N}_{E}\otimes\hat{N}_{A}]

\displaystyle-\log_{2}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)\log_{2}\left(1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}\right)N_{s}

\displaystyle-\log_{2}\left(\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1}\right)\log_{2}\left(1+N_{s}\right)(1-\lambda)N_{s}

\displaystyle+\log_{2}\left(1+N_{s}\right)\log_{2}\left(1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}\right)\,.

Hence, we just need to evaluate the term
\Tr[\Psi_{AE}\hat{N}_{A}\otimes\hat{N}_{E}]
. We have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Tr[\Psi_{AE}\hat{N}_{A}\otimes\hat{N}_{E}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}nl\Tr[\Psi_{AE}\ket{n}\!\!\bra{n}_{A}\otimes\ket{l}\!\!\bra{l}_{E}]

(291)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\scriptsize\mbox{(i)}}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{N_{s}+1}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{l=0}^{n}nl\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{n}\binom{n}{l}\lambda^{n-l}(1-\lambda)^{l}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\scriptsize\mbox{(ii)}}}}{{=}}\frac{1-\lambda}{N_{s}+1}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n^{2}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{n}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\scriptsize\mbox{(iii)}}}}{{=}}(1-\lambda)N_{s}(2N_{s}+1)\,,

where in (i) we employed Lemma
59
, in (ii) we used that the known expression for the mean value of the binomial distribution, and in (iii)
we exploited that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}n^{2}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{n}=N_{s}(N_{s}+1)(2N_{s}+1)\,.

(292)

Hence, by substituting in (
290
), one can easily show that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Tr[\Psi_{AE}(\log_{2}\Psi_{A})(\log_{2}\Psi_{E})]-h(N_{s})h((1-\lambda)N_{s})=(1-\lambda)N_{s}(1+N_{s})\log_{2}\left(\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}}\right)\log_{2}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{1+N_{s}}\right)\,.

(293)

Hence, by employing (
287
), one can prove the expression of
V(B|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}}
in (
281
).

Now, let us prove the expression of
V(A|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}}
in (
281
). As seen above in (
283
), it holds that
V(A|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}}=V(A|B)_{\Psi_{ABE}}
and that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptV(A|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}}

\displaystyle=\Tr[\Psi_{B}(\log_{2}\Psi_{B})^{2}]-\Tr[\Psi_{B}\log_{2}\Psi_{B}]^{2}

(294)

\displaystyle+\Tr[\Psi_{E}(\log_{2}\Psi_{E})^{2}]-\Tr[\Psi_{E}\log_{2}\Psi_{E}]^{2}

\displaystyle-2\Tr[\Psi_{BE}(\log_{2}\Psi_{B})(\log_{2}\Psi_{E})]+2\Tr[\Psi_{E}\log_{2}\Psi_{E}]\Tr[\Psi_{B}\log_{2}\Psi_{B}]\,.

Since

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Psi_{B}

\displaystyle=\tau_{\lambda N_{s}}\,,

(295)

\displaystyle\Psi_{E}

\displaystyle=\tau_{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}\,,

as established by Lemma
59
, we can (
96
) to obtain that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt-\Tr[\Psi_{B}\log_{2}\Psi_{B}]

\displaystyle=h(\lambda N_{s})\,,

(296)

\displaystyle-\Tr[\Psi_{E}\log_{2}\Psi_{E}]

\displaystyle=h\!\left((1-\lambda)N_{s}\right)\,,

where
h(x)\coloneqq(x+1)\log_{2}(x+1)-x\log_{2}x
. Furthermore, the first two terms in
294
are the entropy variances of
\Psi_{B}
and
\Psi_{E}
, which are both thermal states. Using (
97
), these terms can be expressed as

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Tr[\Psi_{B}(\log_{2}\Psi_{B})^{2}]-\Tr[\Psi_{B}\log_{2}\Psi_{B}]^{2}

\displaystyle=\lambda N_{s}(\lambda N_{s}+1)\log_{2}\left(1+\frac{1}{\lambda N_{s}}\right)^{2}\,,

(297)

\displaystyle\Tr[\Psi_{E}(\log_{2}\Psi_{E})^{2}]-\Tr[\Psi_{E}\log_{2}\Psi_{E}]^{2}

\displaystyle=(1-\lambda)N_{s}\left[(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1\right]\log_{2}\left(1+\frac{1}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}\right)^{2}\,.

Hence, we have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptV(A|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}}

\displaystyle=\lambda N_{s}(\lambda N_{s}+1)\log_{2}\left(1+\frac{1}{\lambda N_{s}}\right)^{2}

(298)

\displaystyle(1-\lambda)N_{s}\left[(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1\right]\log_{2}\left(1+\frac{1}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-2\left[\Tr[\Psi_{BE}(\log_{2}\Psi_{B})(\log_{2}\Psi_{E})]-h(\lambda N_{s})h((1-\lambda)N_{s})\right]

To complete the calculation of all the terms in (
287
), we only need to calculate the term
\Tr[\Psi_{BE}(\log_{2}\Psi_{B})(\log_{2}\Psi_{E})]
. As done above, we have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\log_{2}\Psi_{B}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\left(\frac{\lambda N_{s}}{\lambda N_{s}+1}\right)\hat{N}_{B}-\log_{2}(1+\lambda N_{s})\mathbb{1}_{B}\,,

(299)

\displaystyle\log_{2}\Psi_{E}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\left(\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1}\right)\hat{N}_{E}-\log_{2}(1+(1-\lambda)N_{s})\mathbb{1}_{E}\,,

and hence

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Tr[\Psi_{BE}(\log_{2}\Psi_{B})(\log_{2}\Psi_{E})]

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\left(\frac{\lambda N_{s}}{\lambda N_{s}+1}\right)\log_{2}\left(\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1}\right)\Tr[\Psi_{BE}\hat{N}_{B}\otimes\hat{N}_{E}]

(300)

\displaystyle-\log_{2}\left(\frac{\lambda N_{s}}{\lambda N_{s}+1}\right)\log_{2}\left(1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}\right)\Tr[\Psi_{B}\hat{N}_{B}]

\displaystyle-\log_{2}\left(\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1}\right)\log_{2}\left(1+\lambda N_{s}\right)\Tr[\Psi_{E}\hat{N}_{E}]

\displaystyle+\log_{2}\left(1+\lambda N_{s}\right)\log_{2}\left(1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}\right)

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\left(\frac{\lambda N_{s}}{\lambda N_{s}+1}\right)\log_{2}\left(\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1}\right)\Tr[\Psi_{BE}\hat{N}_{B}\otimes\hat{N}_{E}]

\displaystyle-\log_{2}\left(\frac{\lambda N_{s}}{\lambda N_{s}+1}\right)\log_{2}\left(1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}\right)\lambda N_{s}

\displaystyle-\log_{2}\left(\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1}\right)\log_{2}\left(1+\lambda N_{s}\right)(1-\lambda)N_{s}

\displaystyle+\log_{2}\left(1+\lambda N_{s}\right)\log_{2}\left(1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}\right)\,.

Hence, we just need to evaluate the term
\Tr[\Psi_{BE}\hat{N}_{B}\otimes\hat{N}_{E}]
. By employing Lemma
59
, we have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Tr[\Psi_{BE}\hat{N}_{B}\otimes\hat{N}_{E}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}l\Tr[\Psi_{AE}\hat{N}_{B}\otimes\ket{l}\!\!\bra{l}_{E}]

(301)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\scriptsize\mbox{(iv)}}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{N_{s}+1}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{l=0}^{n}(n-l)l\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{n}\binom{n}{l}\lambda^{n-l}(1-\lambda)^{l}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\scriptsize\mbox{(v)}}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{N_{s}+1}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{n}\left[n^{2}(1-\lambda)-\left(n\lambda(1-\lambda)+n^{2}(1-\lambda)^{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\scriptsize\mbox{(vi)}}}}{{=}}\frac{\lambda(1-\lambda)}{N_{s}+1}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{n}(n^{2}-n)

\displaystyle=2\lambda(1-\lambda)N_{s}^{2}\,,

where in (v) and in (vi) we used standard moment evaluations for binomial and geometric distributions. Hence, by substituting in (
300
), one can easily show that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Tr[\Psi_{BE}(\log_{2}\Psi_{B})(\log_{2}\Psi_{E})]-h(\lambda N_{s})h((1-\lambda)N_{s})=(1-\lambda)\lambda N_{s}^{2}\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}}\right)\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{\lambda N_{s}}{1+\lambda N_{s}}\right)\,.

(302)

Hence, by employing (
298
) one can prove the expression of
V(A|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}}
in (
281
). Both limits in (
282
) are then easy to obtain.
∎

The following lemma establishes that
V(B|E)_{\Psi^{(k)}}
and
V(A|E)_{\Psi^{(k)}}
converge to
V(B|E)_{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}
and
V(A|E)_{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}
, respectively.

Lemma 61
.

Let
\lambda\in(0,1),N_{s}>0
, and let
\Psi_{ABE}\coloneqq\ket{\Psi}\!\!\bra{\Psi}_{ABE}
, where

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\ket{\Psi}_{ABE}\coloneqq\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes U_{\lambda}^{BE}\,\ket{\psi_{N_{s}}}_{AB}\otimes\ket{0}_{E}\,,

(303)

is the state defined in (
276
). Moreover, let
\Psi_{ABE}^{(k)}\coloneqq\ket{\Psi^{(k)}}\!\!\bra{\Psi^{(k)}}_{ABE}
, where

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\ket{\Psi^{(k)}}_{ABE}\coloneqq\frac{P_{k}^{A}\ket{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}_{ABE}}{\sqrt{\Tr[P_{k}\tau_{N_{s}}]}}\,.

(304)

is the state defined in (
263
). Then, it holds that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}V(A|E)_{\Psi^{(k)}}

\displaystyle=V(A|E)_{\Psi}\,,

(305)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}V(B|E)_{\Psi^{(k)}}

\displaystyle=V(B|E)_{\Psi}\,.

Proof.

From the definition of conditional entropy variance, it follows that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptV(A|E)_{\Psi}

\displaystyle=\Tr\left[\Psi_{AE}\left(\log_{2}\Psi_{AE}-\log_{2}\Psi_{E}\right)^{2}\right]-[S(A|E)_{\Psi}]^{2}

(306)

\displaystyle=\Tr[\Psi_{AE}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi_{AE}]+\Tr[\Psi_{E}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi_{E}]-2\Tr[\Psi_{AE}\log_{2}\Psi_{AE}\log_{2}\Psi_{E}]-[S(A|E)_{\Psi}]^{2}

and that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptV(A|E)_{\Psi^{(k)}}=\Tr[\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}]+\Tr[\Psi^{(k)}_{E}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{E}]-2\Tr[\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}\log_{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}\log_{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{E}]-[S(A|E)_{\Psi^{(k)}}]^{2}\,.

(307)

Hence, in order to show that
\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}V(A|E)_{\Psi^{(k)}}=V(A|E)_{\Psi}
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}S(A|E)_{\Psi^{(k)}}

\displaystyle=S(A|E)_{\Psi}\,,

(308)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\Tr[\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}]

\displaystyle=\Tr[\Psi_{AE}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi_{AE}]\,,

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\Tr[\Psi^{(k)}_{E}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{E}]

\displaystyle=\Tr[\Psi_{E}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi_{E}]\,,

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\Tr[\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}\log_{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}\log_{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{E}]

\displaystyle=\Tr[\Psi_{AE}\log_{2}\Psi_{AE}\log_{2}\Psi_{E}]\,.

The first limit in (
308
) follows from the same reasoning used to prove (
207
), which leveraged the convergence of
\Psi^{(k)}
to
\Psi
in trace norm and the continuity of conditional entropy with respect to the trace norm over the set of states with bounded mean photon number
[
78
]
.

Now, let us prove the second limit in (
308
), i.e.
\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\Tr[\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}]=\Tr[\Psi_{AE}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi_{AE}]
. This is equivalent to show that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\Tr[\Psi^{(k)}_{B}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{B}]=\Tr[\Psi_{B}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi_{B}]\,.

(309)

Thanks to Lemma
59
, we have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Psi_{B}=\tau_{\lambda N_{s}}=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}q_{\infty,l}\ket{l}\!\!\bra{l}\,,

(310)

where we introduced

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptq_{\infty,l}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\lambda N_{s}+1}\left(\frac{\lambda N_{s}}{\lambda N_{s}+1}\right)^{l}\,.

(311)

Similarly to the proof of Lemma
59
, we have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\ket{\Psi^{(k)}}_{ABE}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Tr[P_{k}\tau_{N_{s}}]}}\sum_{n=0}^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{s}+1}}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{n/2}(-1)^{l}\sqrt{\binom{n}{l}\lambda^{n-l}(1-\lambda)^{l}}\ket{n}_{A}\otimes\ket{n-l}_{B}\otimes\ket{l}_{E}\,.

(312)

Hence, we have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Psi^{(k)}_{B}=\frac{1}{p_{k}}\sum_{l=0}^{k}q_{k,l}\ket{l}\!\!\bra{l}\,,

(313)

where we introduced

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptq_{k,l}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{n=l}^{k}\frac{1}{N_{s}+1}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{n}\binom{n}{l}\lambda^{l}(1-\lambda)^{n-l}\,,

(314)

\displaystyle p_{k}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Tr[p_{k}\tau_{N_{s}}]=\frac{1}{N_{s}+1}\sum_{n=0}^{k}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{n}=1-\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{k+1}\,.

Note that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Tr[\Psi^{(k)}_{B}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{B}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{q_{k,l}}{p_{k}}\left(\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,l}}{p_{k}}\right)^{2}

(315)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{p_{k}}}\sum_{l=0}^{k}q_{k,l}\left(\log_{2}^{2}q_{k,l}-2\log_{2}p_{k}\log_{2}q_{k,l}+\log_{2}^{2}p_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{p_{k}}}\sum_{l=0}^{k}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{k,l}-2\log_{2}p_{k}\sum_{l=0}^{k}\frac{q_{k,l}}{p_{k}}\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,l}}{p_{k}}-\log_{2}^{2}p_{k}\,,

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{p_{k}}}\sum_{l=0}^{k}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{k,l}+2(\log_{2}p_{k})S(\Psi^{(k)}_{B})-\log_{2}^{2}p_{k}\,.

By exploiting the facts that (i)
\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}p_{k}=1
, (ii) the state
\Psi^{(k)}_{B}
converges to
\Psi_{B}
in trace norm (as proved in the proof of Theorem
45
), which has finite entropy (specifically,
S(\Psi_{B})=S(\tau_{\lambda N_{s}})=h(\lambda N_{s})
), and (iii) the von Neumann entropy is continuous with respect the trace norm over the set of states having bounded mean photon number
[
78
]
, it follows that the term
2(\log_{2}p_{k})S(\Psi^{(k)}_{B})-\log_{2}^{2}p_{k}
converges to zero as
k\rightarrow\infty
. Hence, to complete the proof of
\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\Tr[\Psi^{(k)}_{B}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{B}]=\Tr[\Psi_{B}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi_{B}]
, we just need to prove that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{l=0}^{k}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{k,l}=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}q_{\infty,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{\infty,l}\,.

(316)

Let us fix
l
and
k
such that
l\leq k
. Note that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda N_{s}+1}{N_{s}+1}\left(\frac{\lambda N_{s}}{\lambda N_{s}+1}\right)^{-l}\sum_{n=l}^{k}\binom{n}{l}\lambda^{l}(1-\lambda)^{n-l}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{n}

(317)

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda N_{s}+1}{N_{s}+1}\left(\frac{\lambda N_{s}}{\lambda N_{s}+1}\right)^{-l}\sum_{n=0}^{k-l}\binom{n+l}{l}\lambda^{l}(1-\lambda)^{n}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{n+l}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{k-l}\binom{n+l}{l}\left(\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{n}\left(\frac{\lambda N_{s}+1}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{l+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{k-l}N\!B\left(n;l+1,\frac{\lambda N_{s}+1}{N_{s}+1}\right)

\displaystyle=1-\sum_{n=k-l+1}^{\infty}N\!B\left(n;l+1,\frac{\lambda N_{s}+1}{N_{s}+1}\right)\,,

where we introduced the
negative binomial distribution
defined as

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptN\!B(n;r,p)\coloneqq\binom{n+r-1}{n}(1-p)^{n}p^{r}

(318)

for all
p\in[0,1],n\in\mathbb{N},r\in\mathbb{N}
.
Its moment generating function is known to be

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}N\!B(n;r,p)e^{tn}=\left(\frac{p}{1-(1-p)e^{t}}\right)^{r}\,.

(319)

for
t<\ln\frac{1}{1-p}
. Consequently, by applying a Chernoff bound approach, we have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\sum_{n=m}^{\infty}N\!B(n;r,p)\leq e^{-tm}\sum_{n=m}^{\infty}e^{tn}N\!B(n;r,p)\leq e^{-tm}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}e^{tn}N\!B(n;r,p)=e^{-tm}\left(\frac{p}{1-(1-p)e^{t}}\right)^{r}

(320)

for all
t
such that
e^{t}<\frac{1}{1-p}
. Let
m\geq 1
and let us choose
t
such that
e^{t}=\frac{m}{m+r}\frac{1}{1-p}
. Hence, it holds that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\sum_{n=m}^{\infty}N\!B(n;r,p)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{p^{r}(1-p)^{m}}{(\frac{r}{m+r})^{r}(1-\frac{r}{m+r})^{m}}

(321)

\displaystyle=e^{r\left(\ln p-\ln(\frac{r}{m+r})\right)+m\left(\ln(1-p)-\ln(1-\frac{r}{m+r})\right)}

\displaystyle=e^{-(m+r)\left[\frac{r}{m+r}\left(\ln(\frac{r}{m+r})-\ln p\right)+(1-\frac{r}{m+r})\left(\ln(1-\frac{r}{m+r})-\ln(1-p)\right)\right]}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\scriptsize\mbox{(i)}}}}{{\leq}}e^{-2(m+r)(p-\frac{r}{m+r})^{2}}\,,

where in (i) we exploited the elementary inequality

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptp\ln\left(\frac{p}{q}\right)+(1-p)\ln\left(\frac{1-p}{1-q}\right)\geq 2(p-q)^{2}\quad\forall\,p,q\in(0,1)\,,

(322)

which can be proved e.g. exploiting Pinsker’s inequality. Hence, by employing (
317
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}\geq 1-e^{-2(k+2)(p-\frac{l+1}{k+2})^{2}}\,,

(323)

where we defined
p\coloneqq\frac{\lambda N_{s}+1}{N_{s}+1}
. Additionally, note that it always holds that
\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}\leq 1
(this can be proved e.g. exploiting (
317
)).

Now, let us proceed with the proof of
\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{l=0}^{k}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{k,l}=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}q_{\infty,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{\infty,l}
. Let us split the sum as

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\sum_{l=0}^{k}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{k,l}=\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{k,l}+\sum_{l=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{k,l}\,,

(324)

In this way, in the first sum we can assume that
(p-\frac{l+1}{k+2})^{2}\geq p^{2}/4
and thus the following lower bound holds

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}\geq 1-e^{-(k+2)p^{2}/2}\,.

(325)

Let us prove that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{k,l}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}q_{\infty,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{\infty,l}\,,

(326)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{l=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{k,l}

\displaystyle=0\,.

Let us begin with the first limit. Let us write

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{k,l}=\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}+2\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}q_{k,l}\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}\log_{2}q_{\infty,l}+\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{\infty,l}\,,

(327)

For the first term, we have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt0\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}q_{\infty,l}\log_{2}^{2}\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}

(328)

\displaystyle\leq\log_{2}^{2}\left(1-e^{-(k+2)p^{2}/2}\right)\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}q_{\infty,l}

\displaystyle\leq\log_{2}^{2}\left(1-e^{-(k+2)p^{2}/2}\right)\xrightarrow{k\rightarrow\infty}0\,,

For the second term, we obtain that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt0\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}q_{k,l}\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}\log_{2}q_{\infty,l}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}q_{\infty,l}\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}\log_{2}q_{\infty,l}

(329)

\displaystyle\leq\log_{2}\left(1-e^{-(k+2)p^{2}/2}\right)\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}q_{\infty,l}\log_{2}q_{\infty,l}

\displaystyle\leq\log_{2}\left(1-e^{-(k+2)p^{2}/2}\right)\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}q_{\infty,l}\log_{2}q_{\infty,l}

\displaystyle=-\log_{2}\left(1-e^{-(k+2)p^{2}/2}\right)S(\Psi_{B})\xrightarrow{k\rightarrow\infty}0\,,

where in the last line we used that
S(\Psi_{B})=h(\lambda N_{s})
is finite. Finally, for the third term, we observe that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{\infty,l}=\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}q_{\infty,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{\infty,l}-\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}(q_{\infty,l}-q_{k,l})\log_{2}^{2}q_{\infty,l}

(330)

and that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}q_{\infty,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{\infty,l}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}q_{\infty,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{\infty,l}\,.

(331)

Additionally, it holds that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt0\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}(q_{\infty,l}-q_{k,l})\log_{2}^{2}q_{\infty,l}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}q_{\infty,l}(1-\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}})\log_{2}^{2}q_{\infty,l}

(332)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-(k+2)p^{2}/2}\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}q_{\infty,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{\infty,l}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-(k+2)p^{2}/2}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}q_{\infty,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{\infty,l}

\displaystyle=e^{-(k+2)p^{2}/2}\Tr[\Psi_{B}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi_{B}]\xrightarrow{k\rightarrow\infty}0\,,

where in the last line we used that
\Tr[\Psi_{B}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi_{B}]
is finite (see e.g.
297
). Hence, we proved that the third term satisfies

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{l=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{\infty,l}=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}q_{\infty,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{\infty,l}\,,

(333)

and thus the proof of the first limit in (
326
) is complete. Let us now proceed with the proof of the second one, i.e.
\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{l=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{k,l}=0
. Similarly as above, it holds that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\sum_{l=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{k,l}=\sum_{l=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}+2\sum_{l=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}q_{k,l}\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}\log_{2}q_{\infty,l}+\sum_{l=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{\infty,l}\,,

(334)

To show that each of these terms goes to zero, we use the fact that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{k-l}\binom{n+l}{l}\left(\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{n}\left(\frac{\lambda N_{s}+1}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{l+1}\geq\left(\frac{\lambda N_{s}+1}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{l+1}=p^{l+1}\,,

(335)

where the first inequality follows from (
317
).
For the first term, we have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt0\leq\sum_{l=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}q_{\infty,l}\log_{2}^{2}\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}

(336)

\displaystyle\leq(\log_{2}p)^{2}\sum_{l=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}q_{\infty,l}(l+1)^{2}\xrightarrow{k\rightarrow\infty}0\,,

where in the last line we used that
\Tr[\Psi_{B}(a^{\dagger}a)^{2}]
is finite (recall that
\Psi_{B}=\tau_{\lambda N_{s}}
is a thermal state). Similarly, for the second term, we obtain that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt0\leq\sum_{l=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}q_{k,l}\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}\log_{2}q_{\infty,l}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}q_{\infty,l}\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}\log_{2}q_{\infty,l}

(337)

\displaystyle\leq(\log_{2}p)\sum_{l=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}q_{\infty,l}(l+1)\log_{2}q_{\infty,l}

\displaystyle=(\log_{2}p)\sum_{l=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}q_{\infty,l}(l+1)\log_{2}\left[\frac{1}{\lambda N_{s}+1}\left(\frac{\lambda N_{s}}{\lambda N_{s}+1}\right)^{l}\right]\xrightarrow{k\rightarrow\infty}0\,,

where in the last line we used that both
\Tr[\Psi_{B}(a^{\dagger}a)^{2}]
and
\Tr[\Psi_{B}a^{\dagger}a]
are finite, as
\Psi_{B}=\tau_{\lambda N_{s}}
is a thermal state. Finally, for the third term, we have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt0\leq\sum_{l=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{\infty,l}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}q_{\infty,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{\infty,l}\xrightarrow{k\rightarrow\infty}0\,,

(338)

where in the last line we exploited that
\Tr[\Psi_{B}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi_{B}]
is finite (see e.g.
297
). Consequently, the proof of the second limit in (
326
) is complete. To summarise, we have proved that
\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{l=0}^{k}q_{k,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{k,l}=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}q_{\infty,l}\log_{2}^{2}q_{\infty,l}
, which implies the validity of the second limit in (
308
).

The proof of the third limit in (
308
), i.e.
\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\Tr[\Psi^{(k)}_{E}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{E}]=\Tr[\Psi_{E}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi_{E}]
, is completely analogous to the proof of
\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\Tr[\Psi^{(k)}_{B}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{B}]=\Tr[\Psi_{B}\log_{2}^{2}\Psi_{B}]
.

Let us now show that the fourth limit in (
308
), i.e.
\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\Tr[\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}\log_{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}\log_{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{E}]=\Tr[\Psi_{AE}\log_{2}\Psi_{AE}\log_{2}\Psi_{E}]
. First, note that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Tr[\Psi_{AE}\log_{2}\Psi_{AE}\log_{2}\Psi_{E}]

\displaystyle=\Tr[\Psi_{ABE}\log_{2}\Psi_{AE}\log_{2}\Psi_{E}]

(339)

\displaystyle=\Tr[\Psi_{ABE}\log_{2}\Psi_{B}\log_{2}\Psi_{E}]

\displaystyle=\Tr[\Psi_{BE}\log_{2}\Psi_{B}\log_{2}\Psi_{E}]\,,

where in the last line we used the general fact that for any bipartite state
\ket{\psi}_{S_{1}S_{2}}
on systems
S_{1},S_{2}
it holds that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\left(\Tr_{S_{1}}\ket{\psi}\!\!\bra{\psi}_{S_{1}S_{2}}\otimes\mathbb{1}_{S_{2}}\right)\ket{\psi}_{S_{1}S_{2}}=\left(\mathbb{1}_{S_{1}}\otimes\Tr_{S_{2}}\ket{\psi}\!\!\bra{\psi}_{S_{1}S_{2}}\right)\ket{\psi}_{S_{1}S_{2}}\,,

(340)

which can be shown e.g. via Schmidt decomposition. Analogously, it holds that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Tr[\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}\log_{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}\log_{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{E}]=\Tr[\Psi^{(k)}_{BE}\log_{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{B}\log_{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{E}]\,.

(341)

Hence, we need to prove that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\Tr[\Psi^{(k)}_{BE}\log_{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{B}\log_{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{E}]=\Tr[\Psi_{BE}\log_{2}\Psi_{B}\log_{2}\Psi_{E}]\,.

(342)

From (
312
) it follows that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Psi^{(k)}_{B}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p_{k}}\sum_{l=0}^{k}q_{k,l}\ket{l}\!\!\bra{l}\,,

(343)

\displaystyle\Psi^{(k)}_{E}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p_{k}}\sum_{l=0}^{k}q^{\prime}_{k,l}\ket{l}\!\!\bra{l}\,,

where we introduced

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptq_{k,l}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{n=l}^{k}\frac{1}{N_{s}+1}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{n}\binom{n}{l}\lambda^{l}(1-\lambda)^{n-l}\,,

(344)

\displaystyle q^{\prime}_{k,l}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{n=l}^{k}\frac{1}{N_{s}+1}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{n}\binom{n}{l}(1-\lambda)^{l}\lambda^{n-l}\,,

\displaystyle p_{k}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Tr[p_{k}\tau_{N_{s}}]=\frac{1}{N_{s}+1}\sum_{n=0}^{k}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{n}=1-\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{k+1}\,.

Moreover, it holds that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Psi^{(k)}_{BE}=\frac{1}{p_{k}}\sum_{n=0}^{k}\sum_{l_{1},l_{2}=0}^{n}\frac{1}{N_{s}+1}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{n}(-1)^{l_{1}+l_{2}}\sqrt{\binom{n}{l_{1}}\binom{n}{l_{2}}\lambda^{2n-l_{1}-l_{2}}(1-\lambda)^{l_{1}+l_{2}}}\ket{n-l_{1}}\!\!\bra{n-l_{2}}_{B}\otimes\ket{l_{1}}\!\!\bra{l_{2}}_{E}\,.

(345)

Consequently, we obtain that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Tr[\Psi^{(k)}_{BE}\log_{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{B}\log_{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{E}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\log_{2}\frac{q^{\prime}_{k,l}}{p_{k}}\Tr[\Psi^{(k)}_{BE}\log_{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{B}\ket{l}\!\!\bra{l}_{E}]

(346)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{k}\sum_{n=l}^{k}\frac{\tilde{q}_{l,n}}{p_{k}}\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,n-l}}{p_{k}}\log_{2}\frac{q^{\prime}_{k,l}}{p_{k}}\,,

where we defined

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\tilde{q}_{l,n}\coloneqq\frac{1}{N_{s}+1}\left(\frac{N_{s}}{N_{s}+1}\right)^{n}\binom{n}{l}\lambda^{n-l}(1-\lambda)^{l}\,.

(347)

Moreover, it can be easily shown that (e.g. by considering the partial traces of
\Psi_{BE}
)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\sum_{n=l}^{k}\tilde{q}_{n-l,n}

\displaystyle=q_{k,l}\,,

(348)

\displaystyle\sum_{n=l}^{k}\tilde{q}_{l,n}

\displaystyle=q^{\prime}_{k,l}\,.

Analogously, it holds that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Tr[\Psi_{BE}\log_{2}\Psi_{B}\log_{2}\Psi_{E}]=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n=l}^{\infty}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}q_{\infty,n-l}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{\infty,l}\,,

(349)

where

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptq_{\infty,l}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{\lambda N_{s}+1}\left(\frac{\lambda N_{s}}{\lambda N_{s}+1}\right)^{l}\,,

(350)

\displaystyle q^{\prime}_{\infty,l}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1}\left(\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1}\right)^{l}\,.

Hence, we have to show that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{l=0}^{k}\sum_{n=l}^{k}\frac{\tilde{q}_{l,n}}{p_{k}}\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,n-l}}{p_{k}}\log_{2}\frac{q^{\prime}_{k,l}}{p_{k}}=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n=l}^{\infty}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}q_{\infty,n-l}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{\infty,l}\,.

(351)

Similarly to what we did in (
315
), by exploiting the facts that (i)
\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}p_{k}=1
, (ii) the state
\Psi^{(k)}_{B}
(resp.
\Psi^{(k)}_{E}
) converges to
\Psi_{B}
(resp.
\Psi^{(k)}_{E}
) in trace norm (as proved in the proof of Theorem
45
), which has finite entropy, and (iii) the von Neumann entropy is continuous with respect the trace norm over the set of states having bounded mean photon number
[
78
]
, it follows that we just need to prove that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{l=0}^{k}\sum_{n=l}^{k}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}q_{k,n-l}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{k,l}=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n=l}^{\infty}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}q_{\infty,n-l}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{\infty,l}\,.

(352)

Moreover, we have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\sum_{l=0}^{k}\sum_{n=l}^{k}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}q_{k,n-l}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{k,l}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{k}\sum_{n=l}^{k}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,n-l}}{q_{\infty,n-l}}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{k,l}

(353)

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=0}^{k}\sum_{n=l}^{k}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}q_{\infty,n-l}\log_{2}\frac{q^{\prime}_{k,l}}{q^{\prime}_{\infty,l}}+\sum_{l=0}^{k}\sum_{n=l}^{k}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}q_{\infty,n-l}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{\infty,l}\,.

Since the last term converges to
\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n=l}^{\infty}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}q_{\infty,n-l}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{\infty,l}
as
k\rightarrow\infty
, we just need to show that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{l=0}^{k}\sum_{n=l}^{k}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,n-l}}{q_{\infty,n-l}}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{k,l}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(354)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{l=0}^{k}\sum_{n=l}^{k}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}q_{\infty,n-l}\log_{2}\frac{q^{\prime}_{k,l}}{q^{\prime}_{\infty,l}}

\displaystyle=0\,.

Let us prove the first limit. As we saw above, by defining
p\coloneqq\frac{\lambda N_{s}+1}{N_{s}+1}
, we have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}

\displaystyle\geq 1-e^{-(k+2)p^{2}/2}\quad\forall\,l\leq\left\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\right\rfloor+1\,,

(355)

\displaystyle\frac{q_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}

\displaystyle\geq p^{l+1}\,,

\displaystyle q_{k,l}

\displaystyle\leq q_{\infty,l}\,,

\displaystyle\sum_{n=l}^{k}\tilde{q}_{n-l,n}

\displaystyle=q_{k,l}\,.

Analogously, by defining
p^{\prime}\coloneqq\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1}{N_{s}+1}
, we have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\frac{q^{\prime}_{k,l}}{q^{\prime}_{\infty,l}}

\displaystyle\geq 1-e^{-(k+2)p^{\prime 2}/2}\quad\forall\,l\leq\left\lfloor\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}(k+2)\right\rfloor+1\,,

(356)

\displaystyle\frac{q^{\prime}_{k,l}}{q_{\infty,l}}

\displaystyle\geq p^{\prime l+1}\,,

\displaystyle q^{\prime}_{k,l}

\displaystyle\leq q^{\prime}_{\infty,l}\,,

\displaystyle\sum_{n=l}^{k}\tilde{q}_{l,n}

\displaystyle=q^{\prime}_{k,l}\,.

With the relations in (
355
) and (
356
) at hand, we obtain that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt0

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{k}\sum_{n=l}^{k}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,n-l}}{q_{\infty,n-l}}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{k,l}

(357)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,n-l}}{q_{\infty,n-l}}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{k,l}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,n-l}}{q_{\infty,n-l}}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{k,l}+\sum_{n=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,n-l}}{q_{\infty,n-l}}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{k,l}

\displaystyle\leq\log_{2}\left(1-e^{-(k+2)p^{2}/2}\right)\sum_{n=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{k,l}+\sum_{n=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,n-l}}{q_{\infty,n-l}}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{k,l}

\displaystyle\leq\log_{2}\left(1-e^{-(k+2)p^{2}/2}\right)\sum_{n=0}^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{k,l}+\sum_{n=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,n-l}}{q_{\infty,n-l}}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{k,l}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\left(1-e^{-(k+2)p^{2}/2}\right)\sum_{l=0}^{k}q^{\prime}_{k,l}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{k,l}+\sum_{n=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,n-l}}{q_{\infty,n-l}}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{k,l}

The first term goes to zero as a consequence of the facts that
\log_{2}\left(1-e^{-(k+2)p^{2}/2}\right)\xrightarrow{k\rightarrow\infty}0
and that
\sum_{l=0}^{k}q^{\prime}_{k,l}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{k,l}
converges to
S(\Psi_{E})
, which is finite. Let us analyse the second term:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt0

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}\frac{q_{k,n-l}}{q_{\infty,n-l}}\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{k,l}

(358)

\displaystyle\leq\log_{2}p\sum_{n=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}(n-l+1)\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{k,l}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}p\sum_{n=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}(n-l+1)\log_{2}\frac{q^{\prime}_{k,l}}{q^{\prime}_{\infty,l}}+\log_{2}p\sum_{n=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}(n-l+1)\log_{2}q^{\prime}_{\infty,l}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}p\sum_{n=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}(n-l+1)\log_{2}\frac{q^{\prime}_{k,l}}{q^{\prime}_{\infty,l}}+\log_{2}p\sum_{n=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}(n-l+1)\log_{2}\left(\frac{1}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1}\left(\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1}\right)^{l}\right)

\displaystyle\leq(\log_{2}p)^{2}\sum_{n=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}(n-l+1)(l+1)+\log_{2}p\sum_{n=\lfloor\frac{p}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}(n-l+1)\log_{2}\left(\frac{1}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1}\left(\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{(1-\lambda)N_{s}+1}\right)^{l}\right)\,.

These two sums converge to zero because the series

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Tr[\Psi_{BE}\hat{N}_{B}\otimes\hat{N}_{E}]=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{l=n}^{\infty}\tilde{q}_{l,n}(n-l)l

(359)

converges (specifically, it equals
2\lambda(1-\lambda)N_{s}^{2}
), as a consequence of (
301
). Hence, we have proved the first limit in (
354
). Now, let us prove the second limit, i.e.
\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{l=0}^{k}\sum_{n=l}^{k}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}q_{\infty,n-l}\log_{2}\frac{q^{\prime}_{k,l}}{q^{\prime}_{\infty,l}}=0
. Similarly to what we did with the first limit, by exploiting (
355
) and (
356
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt0

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{k}\sum_{n=l}^{k}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}q_{\infty,n-l}\log_{2}\frac{q^{\prime}_{k,l}}{q^{\prime}_{\infty,l}}

(360)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}q_{\infty,n-l}\log_{2}\frac{q^{\prime}_{k,l}}{q^{\prime}_{\infty,l}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}q_{\infty,n-l}\log_{2}\frac{q^{\prime}_{k,l}}{q^{\prime}_{\infty,l}}+\sum_{n=\lfloor\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}q_{\infty,n-l}\log_{2}\frac{q^{\prime}_{k,l}}{q^{\prime}_{\infty,l}}

\displaystyle\leq\log_{2}\left(1-e^{-(k+2)p^{\prime 2}/2}\right)\sum_{n=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}q_{\infty,n-l}+\log_{2}p^{\prime}\sum_{n=\lfloor\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}q_{\infty,n-l}(l+1)

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\left(1-e^{-(k+2)p^{\prime 2}/2}\right)\sum_{n=0}^{\lfloor\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+1}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}\left(\frac{1}{\lambda N_{s}+1}\left(\frac{\lambda N_{s}}{\lambda N_{s}+1}\right)^{n-l}\right)

\displaystyle+\log_{2}p^{\prime}\sum_{n=\lfloor\frac{p^{\prime}}{2}(k+2)\rfloor+2}^{k}\sum_{l=0}^{n}\tilde{q}_{l,n}\log_{2}\left(\frac{1}{\lambda N_{s}+1}\left(\frac{\lambda N_{s}}{\lambda N_{s}+1}\right)^{n-l}\right)(l+1),.

Analogously as above, both these sums converge to zero because the state
\Psi_{BE}
has finite moments. Consequently, we have proved that
\lim\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\Tr[\Psi^{(k)}_{BE}\log_{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{B}\log_{2}\Psi^{(k)}_{E}]=\Tr[\Psi_{BE}\log_{2}\Psi_{B}\log_{2}\Psi_{E}]
. This concludes the proof of
\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}V(A|E)_{\Psi^{(k)}}=V(A|E)_{\Psi}
.
∎

We are now ready to bound the
n
-shot capacities of the pure loss channel. Let us start from the energy-constrained case.

Theorem 62

((Lower bound on the energy-constrained
n
-shot capacities of the pure loss channel via entropy variance approach))
.

Let
N_{s}>0
,
\lambda\in[0,1]
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. The energy-constrained
n
-shot quantum capacity, the energy-constrained
n
-shot two-way quantum capacity, and the energy-constrained
n
-shot secret-key capacity of the pure loss channel
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
can be lower bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptQ^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},N_{s})

\displaystyle\geq n\left(h\!\left(\lambda N_{s}\right)-h\!\left((1-\lambda)N_{s}\right)\right)-4\sqrt{\frac{nV(A|E)_{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}}{\varepsilon}}-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{23}(32-\varepsilon)^{2}}{(16-\varepsilon)\varepsilon^{6}}\right)\,,

(361)

\displaystyle K^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},N_{s})
