\displaystyle\geq Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},N_{s})

\displaystyle\geq n\left(h\!\left(N_{s}\right)-h\!\left((1-\lambda)N_{s}\right)\right)-\sqrt{\frac{2nV(B|E)_{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}}-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{6}\,3\,(4-\sqrt{\varepsilon})^{2}}{(2-\sqrt{\varepsilon})\varepsilon^{3}}\right)\,,

where
h(x)\coloneqq(x+1)\log_{2}(x+1)-x\log_{2}x
and where the conditional entropy variances are given by

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptV(A|E)_{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=\lambda N_{s}(1+\lambda N_{s})\left(\log_{2}\frac{\lambda N_{s}}{1+\lambda N_{s}}\right)^{2}+(1-\lambda)N_{s}(1+(1-\lambda)N_{s})\left(\log_{2}\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}}\right)^{2}

(362)

\displaystyle-2(1-\lambda)\lambda N_{s}^{2}\log_{2}\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}}\log_{2}\frac{\lambda N_{s}}{1+\lambda N_{s}}\,,

\displaystyle V(B|E)_{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=N_{s}(1+N_{s})\left(\log_{2}\frac{N_{s}}{1+N_{s}}\right)^{2}+(1-\lambda)N_{s}(1+(1-\lambda)N_{s})\left(\log_{2}\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-2(1-\lambda)N_{s}(1+N_{s})\left(\log_{2}\frac{(1-\lambda)N_{s}}{1+(1-\lambda)N_{s}}\right)\left(\log_{2}\frac{N_{s}}{1+N_{s}}\right)\,.

Proof.

By exploiting Lemma
57
and Lemma
58
, we have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptQ^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},N_{s})

\displaystyle\geq-nD(\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}\|\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes\Psi^{(k)}_{E})-4\sqrt{\frac{{nV(A|E)_{\Psi^{(k)}}}}{\varepsilon}}-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{23}(32-\varepsilon)^{2}}{(16-\varepsilon)\varepsilon^{6}}\right)\,,

(363)

\displaystyle K^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},N_{s})

\displaystyle\geq Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda},N_{s})

\displaystyle\geq-nD(\Psi^{(k)}_{BE}\|\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes\Psi^{(k)}_{E})-\sqrt{\frac{{2nV(B|E)_{\Psi^{(k)}}}}{\sqrt{\varepsilon}}}-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{6}\,3\,(4-\sqrt{\varepsilon})^{2}}{(2-\sqrt{\varepsilon})\varepsilon^{3}}\right)\,.

The lower bounds in (
361
) follow by taking the limit for
k\rightarrow\infty
of the above lower bounds. Specifically, we do this by exploiting Lemma
61
and the fact, proved in the proof of Theorem
45
(see the first equation of (
205
)), that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}D(\Psi^{(k)}_{AE}\|\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes\Psi^{(k)}_{E})

\displaystyle=D(\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}_{AE}\|\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}_{E})=-I_{c}(A\,\rangle\,B)_{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}=-h(\lambda N_{s})+h((1-\lambda)N_{s})\,,

(364)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}D(\Psi^{(k)}_{BE}\|\mathbb{1}_{B}\otimes\Psi^{(k)}_{E})

\displaystyle=D(\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}_{BE}\|\mathbb{1}_{B}\otimes\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}_{E})=-I_{c}(B\,\rangle\,A)_{\Psi^{(\lambda,N_{s})}}=-h(N_{s})+h((1-\lambda)N_{s})\,,

where the expression of the coherent information quantities are reported in (
121
) and (
123
).
∎

By taking the limit
N_{s}\rightarrow\infty
of (
361
), we can find a lower bound on the (unconstrained)
n
-shot capacities of the pure loss channel.

Theorem 63

((Lower bound on the
n
-shot capacities of the pure loss channel via entropy variance approach))
.

Let
\lambda\in[0,1]
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. The
n
-shot quantum capacity, the
n
-shot two-way quantum capacity, and the
n
-shot secret-key capacity of the pure loss channel
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
can be lower bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptQ^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq nQ(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})-\log_{2}\left(\frac{2^{23}(32-\varepsilon)^{2}}{(16-\varepsilon)\varepsilon^{6}}\right)\,,

(365)

\displaystyle K^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})\geq nQ_{2}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{6}\,3\,(4-\sqrt{\varepsilon})^{2}}{(2-\sqrt{\varepsilon})\varepsilon^{3}}\right)\,,

where

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptQ(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{\lambda}{1-\lambda}\right)&\text{if $\lambda\in(\frac{1}{2},1]$ ,}\\
0&\text{if $\lambda\in[0,\frac{1}{2}]$ ,}\end{cases}

(366)

and
Q_{2}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})=\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\lambda}\right)
are the (asymptotic) quantum capacity and two-way quantum capacity of the pure loss channel, respectively.

D.3
Best lower bounds

Above we have found two different lower bounds on the
n
-shot capacities of the pure loss channel, derived by exploiting two different approaches: the asymptotic equipartition property approach (see Section
D.1
) and the entropy variance approach (see subsection
D.2
). Let us compare these two lower bounds.

The lower bounds derived exploiting the asymptotic equipartition property approach are stated in Theorem
50
and in Theorem
51
, and they read

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptQ^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq nQ(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})-\sqrt{n}4\log_{2}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{1-\lambda}{\lambda}}+\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{1-\lambda}}+1\right)\sqrt{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{9}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)}-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{18}}{3\varepsilon^{4}}\right)\,,

(367)

\displaystyle K^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})\geq nQ_{2}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})-\sqrt{n}4\log_{2}\!\left(\sqrt{1-\lambda}+\sqrt{\frac{1}{1-\lambda}}+1\right)\sqrt{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{8}{\varepsilon}\right)}-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{16}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)\,,

while the lower bounds derived exploiting the relative entropy approach are stated in Theorem
63
and they read

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptQ^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq nQ(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})-\log_{2}\left(\frac{2^{23}(32-\varepsilon)^{2}}{(16-\varepsilon)\varepsilon^{6}}\right)\,,

(368)

\displaystyle K^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})\geq nQ_{2}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{6}\,3\,(4-\sqrt{\varepsilon})^{2}}{(2-\sqrt{\varepsilon})\varepsilon^{3}}\right)\,,

where
Q(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})=\max\!\left(0,\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{\lambda}{1-\lambda}\right)\right)
is the (asymptotic) quantum capacity and
Q_{2}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})=\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\lambda}\right)
is the (asymptotic) two-way quantum capacity. By inspection, we can observe that the best lower bounds are the ones based on the relative entropy approach reported in (
368
), due to the absence of the term proportional to
\sqrt{n}
. Let us report the best lower bound in a quotable theorem.

Theorem 64

((Best lower bounds on the
n
-shot capacities of the pure loss channel))
.

Let
\lambda\in[0,1]
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. The
n
-shot quantum capacity, the
n
-shot two-way quantum capacity, and the
n
-shot secret-key capacity of the pure loss channel
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
can be lower bounded as follows:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptQ^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq nQ(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})-\log_{2}\left(\frac{2^{23}(32-\varepsilon)^{2}}{(16-\varepsilon)\varepsilon^{6}}\right)\,,

(369)

\displaystyle K^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})\geq nQ_{2}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{6}\,3\,(4-\sqrt{\varepsilon})^{2}}{(2-\sqrt{\varepsilon})\varepsilon^{3}}\right)\,,

where
Q(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})=\max\!\left(0,\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{\lambda}{1-\lambda}\right)\right)
and
Q_{2}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})=\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\lambda}\right)
are the (asymptotic) quantum capacity and the (asymptotic) two-way quantum capacity, respectively.

The lower bound on the
n
-shot two-way quantum capacity and secret-key capacity in (
369
) have to be compared with the upper bound established in
[
29
]
:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptQ_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})\leq K^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})\leq n\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\lambda}\right)+\log_{2}6+2\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{1+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\right)\,.

(370)

Consequently, by leveraging (
369
) and (
370
), we conclude that for constant
\varepsilon
, the following holds:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptQ_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})=K^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})=n\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\lambda}\right)+O(1)\,.

(371)

This result proves the conjecture made in
[
47
]
, demonstrating that the upper bound in (
370
) is nearly optimal.

D.4
Channel complexity of the pure loss channel

Traditionally, the analysis of quantum communication over Gaussian channels has been conducted in the
asymptotic
setting, where the number of channel uses approaches infinity and the error in the quantum communication task vanishes. However, due to technological limitations and experimental imperfections, it has become essential to also examine the
non-asymptotic
setting, where the number of channel uses is a fixed (finite) number
n
and the goal is to achieve a
non-zero
error that it is required to be below a certain threshold
\varepsilon
. In this non-asymptotic setting, we address the following questions:

•

How many uses of the pure loss channel
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
are sufficient in order to transmit
k
qubits with an error of at most
\varepsilon
?

•

How many uses of the pure loss channel
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
, assisted by arbitrary LOCCs, are sufficient in order to generate
k
ebits (and hence
k
secret-key bits) with an error of at most
\varepsilon
?

Answering these questions is crucial since the pure loss channel is the most realistic model for optical links, including optical fibres and free-space links.

In the non-asymptotic setting, it is crucial to determine the minimum number
n
of channel uses required to perform a given quantum communication task with an error of at most
\varepsilon
. Determining this minimum
n
is important because it indicates how long one needs to wait in order to complete the quantum communication task. This minimum
n
can be referred to as
channel complexity
, analogous to the concepts of "sample complexity" or "query complexity" well-studied in the quantum learning theory literature
[
46
]
. The channel complexity for qubit distribution, LOCC-assisted entanglement distribution, and LOCC-assisted secret-key distribution over the pure loss channel will be denoted as
n_{Q}(\lambda,\varepsilon,k)
,
n_{Q_{2}}(\lambda,\varepsilon,k)
, and
n_{K}(\lambda,\varepsilon,k)
, respectively. Below, we establish bounds on these channel complexities.

Theorem 65

((Upper bound on the channel complexity of qubit distribution))
.

Let
\lambda\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, and
k\in\mathbb{N}
. It holds that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptn_{Q}(\lambda,\varepsilon,k)\leq\frac{k+\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{23}(32-\varepsilon)^{2}}{(16-\varepsilon)\varepsilon^{6}}\right)}{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{\lambda}{1-\lambda}\right)}\,.

(372)

More explicitly, exploiting a number
\frac{k+\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{23}(32-\varepsilon)^{2}}{(16-\varepsilon)\varepsilon^{6}}\right)}{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{\lambda}{1-\lambda}\right)}
of uses of the pure loss channel
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
is sufficient in order to transmit
k
qubits with an error of at most
\varepsilon
. Here, the error of at most
\varepsilon
is measured in terms of the channel fidelity between the encoded channel and the identity map as defined in Eq. (
37
).

Proof.

In order to transmit
k
qubits with error at most
\varepsilon
across
n
uses of the pure loss channel
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
, it is sufficient to exploit a number
n
of uses of the pure loss channel
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
such that the
n
-shot quantum capacity satisfies
Q^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})\geq k
. Hence, by exploiting Theorem
64
, it thus suffices to require that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptn\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{\lambda}{1-\lambda}\right)-\log_{2}\left(\frac{2^{23}(32-\varepsilon)^{2}}{(16-\varepsilon)\varepsilon^{6}}\right)\geq k\,.

(373)

By solving the above inequality with respect
n
, we conclude the proof.
∎

Let us now state an analogous result for the task of entanglement distribution and secret key distribution.

Theorem 66

((Upper bound on the channel complexity of LOCC-assisted entanglement distribution and secret-key distribution))
.

Let
\lambda\in(0,1)
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, and
k\in\mathbb{N}
. It holds that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptn_{K}(\lambda,\varepsilon,k)\leq n_{Q_{2}}(\lambda,\varepsilon,k)\leq\frac{k+\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{6}\,3\,(4-\sqrt{\varepsilon})^{2}}{(2-\sqrt{\varepsilon})\varepsilon^{3}}\right)}{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\lambda}\right)}\,,

(374)

More explicitly, exploiting a number
\frac{k+\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{6}\,3\,(4-\sqrt{\varepsilon})^{2}}{(2-\sqrt{\varepsilon})\varepsilon^{3}}\right)}{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\lambda}\right)}
of uses of the pure loss channel
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
, together with LOCCs, is sufficient in order to generate
k
ebits (and hence
k
secret-key bits) with an error of at most
\varepsilon
. Here, the error of at most
\varepsilon
is measured in terms of the fidelity between the output state of the protocol and the maximally entangled state as defined in Eq. (
43
).

Proof.

In order to transmit
k
ebits with error at most
\varepsilon
across
n
uses of the pure loss channel
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
, it is sufficient to exploit a number
n
of uses of the pure loss channel
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
such that the
n
-shot two-way quantum capacity satisfies
Q^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})\geq k
. Hence, by exploiting Theorem
64
, it thus suffices to require that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptn\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\lambda}\right)-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{6}\,3\,(4-\sqrt{\varepsilon})^{2}}{(2-\sqrt{\varepsilon})\varepsilon^{3}}\right)\geq k\,,

(375)

By solving the above inequality with respect
n
, we conclude the proof.
∎

By exploiting the upper bound on the
n
-shot capacities reported in (
370
) and proved in
[
29
]
, one can also find a lower bound on the channel complexity of LOCC-assisted entanglement distribution and secret-key distribution over the pure loss channel.

Theorem 67

((Lower bound on the channel complexity of LOCC-assisted entanglement distribution and secret-key distribution))
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\lambda\in(0,1)
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, and
k\in\mathbb{N}
. It holds that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptn_{Q_{2}}(\lambda,\varepsilon,k)\geq n_{K}(\lambda,\varepsilon,k)\geq\frac{k-\log_{2}\left(\frac{6(1+\varepsilon)^{2}}{(1-\varepsilon)^{2}}\right)}{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\lambda}\right)}\,.

(376)

In other words, if
n
uses of the pure loss channel
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}
, together with LOCCs, allows one to generate
k
ebits (and hence
k
secret-key bits) with an error of at most
\varepsilon
, then it must hold that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptn\geq\frac{k-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{6(1+\varepsilon)^{2}}{(1-\varepsilon)^{2}}\right)}{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\lambda}\right)}\,.

(377)

Proof.

By definition of
n
-shot capacities, it must hold that
k\leq K^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\mathcal{E}_{\lambda})
. Moreover, by exploiting the upper bound on the
n
-shot capacities reported in (
370
) and proved in
[
29
]
, we have that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptk\leq n\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\lambda}\right)+\log_{2}6+2\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{1+\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}\right)\,.

(378)

By solving the above inequality with respect
n
, we conclude the proof.
∎

By summarising, the channel complexity of LOCC-assisted entanglement distribution and secret-key distribution over the pure loss channel satisfy

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\frac{k-\log_{2}\left(\frac{6(1+\varepsilon)^{2}}{(1-\varepsilon)^{2}}\right)}{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\lambda}\right)}\leq n_{K}(\lambda,\varepsilon,k)\leq n_{Q_{2}}(\lambda,\varepsilon,k)\leq\frac{k+\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{6}\,3\,(4-\sqrt{\varepsilon})^{2}}{(2-\sqrt{\varepsilon})\varepsilon^{3}}\right)}{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{1}{1-\lambda}\right)}\,.

(379)

Appendix E
Non-asymptotic analysis of quantum communication across the pure amplifier channel

This section aims to establish easily computable lower bounds on the
n
-shot quantum capacity, two-way quantum capacity, and secret-key capacity of the pure amplifier channel
[
1
]
(see Definition
35
for the definition of pure amplifier channel). To establish such lower bounds, we exploit the asymptotic equipartition property approach developed in Section
C
. Specifically, we will evaluate the lower bounds on the
n
-shot capacities proved in Theorem
45
and Theorem
48
for the physically interesting case of the pure amplifier channel
\Phi_{g}
.

Let us apply such theorems by choosing as input state
\Phi_{AA^{\prime}}
the two-mode squeezed vacuum state
\ket{\Psi_{N_{s}}}\!\!\bra{\Psi_{N_{s}}}_{AA^{\prime}}
with local mean photon number equal to
N_{s}
, as defined in Eq. (
94
). Moreover, let us recall that the pure amplifier channel
\Phi_{g}
, defined in Definition
35
, is a single-mode Gaussian channel that admits the following Stinespring dilation:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Phi_{g}(\cdot)=\Tr_{E}\left[U_{g}^{A^{\prime}E\to BE}\big((\cdot)\otimes\ket{0}\!\!\bra{0}_{E}\big)(U_{g}^{A^{\prime}E\to BE})^{\dagger}\right]\,,

(380)

where
U_{g}^{A^{\prime}E\to BE}
denotes the two-mode squeezing unitary defined in Definition
31
. In order to apply Theorem
45
and Theorem
48
, we need to calculate the covariance matrix
V\!\left(\Psi_{ABE}^{(g,N_{s})}\right)
of the tri-partite state

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\Psi_{ABE}^{(g,N_{s})}\coloneqq\left(\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes U_{g}^{BE}\right)\,\left(\ket{\Psi_{N_{s}}}\!\!\bra{\Psi_{N_{s}}}_{AB}\otimes\ket{0}\!\!\bra{0}_{E}\right)\left(\mathbb{1}_{A}\otimes U_{g}^{BE}\right)^{\dagger}\,,

(381)

e.g. with respect the mode-ordering
(A,B,E)
. By exploiting Eq. (
99
) and Eq. (
108
), it follows that
V\!\left(\Psi_{ABE}^{(g,N_{s})}\right)
can be expressed as

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptV\!\left(\Psi_{ABE}^{(g,N_{s})}\right)=\left(\mathbb{1}_{2}\oplus{S}_{g}\right)\left(V(\ket{\Psi_{N_{s}}}\!\!\bra{\Psi_{N_{s}}}_{AB})\oplus V(\ket{0}\!\!\bra{0})\right)\left(\mathbb{1}_{2}\oplus{S}_{g}^{\intercal}\right)\,,

(382)

where:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptS_{g}\coloneqq\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{g}\,\mathbb{1}_{2}\,&\,\sqrt{g-1}\,\sigma_{z}\\
\sqrt{g-1}\,\sigma_{z}\,&\,\sqrt{g}\,\mathbb{1}_{2}\end{pmatrix}

(383)

is the symplectic matrix associated with the two-mode squeezing unitary,
V(\ket{\Psi_{N_{s}}}\!\!\bra{\Psi_{N_{s}}})
is the covariance matrix of the two-mode squeezed vacuum state given in Eq. (
95
), and
V(\ket{0}\!\!\bra{0})=\mathbb{1}_{2}
is the covariance matrix of the vacuum state. By explicitly performing the matrix multiplications, one obtains that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt{V\!\left(\Psi_{ABE}^{(g,N_{s})}\right)=\begin{bmatrix}1+2N_{s}&0&2\sqrt{gN_{s}(1+N_{s})}&0&2\sqrt{(g-1)N_{s}(1+N_{s})}&0\\
0&1+2N_{s}&0&-2\sqrt{gN_{s}(1+N_{s})}&0&2\sqrt{(g-1)N_{s}(1+N_{s})}\\
2\sqrt{gN_{s}(1+N_{s})}&0&-1+g(2N_{s}+1)&0&2\sqrt{g(g-1)}(N_{s}+1)&0\\
0&-2\sqrt{gN_{s}(1+N_{s})}&0&-1+g(2N_{s}+1)&0&-2\sqrt{g(g-1)}(N_{s}+1)\\
2\sqrt{(g-1)N_{s}(1+N_{s})}&0&2\sqrt{g(g-1)}(N_{s}+1)&0&1+2N_{s}(g-1)&0\\
0&2\sqrt{(g-1)N_{s}(1+N_{s})}&0&-2\sqrt{g(g-1)}(N_{s}+1)&0&1+2N_{s}(g-1)\end{bmatrix}\,.}

(384)

Hence, by exploiting Lemma
49
, one can explicitly calculate all the conditional Petz-Rényi entropies involved in Theorem
45
and Theorem
48
. By a simple calculation performed on the Mathematica notebook attached to this manuscript, one obtains:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptH_{1/2}(A|B)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(g,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\left(\frac{\left[2g(1+N_{s})-1+2\sqrt{g(1+N_{s})\big(-1+g(1+N_{s})\big)}\right]\left[2g(1+N_{s})-1-2N_{s}+2\sqrt{(g-1)(1+N_{s})\big(g+(g-1)N_{s}\big)}\right]}{\left[g(2N_{s}+1)+\sqrt{gN_{s}(gN_{s}+1)}+g(N_{s}+1)\sqrt{\frac{(g-1)(N_{s}+1)}{(g-1)N_{s}+g}}\right]^{2}}\right)\,,

(385)

\displaystyle H_{1/2}(A|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(g,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\left(\frac{\left[-1-2N_{s}+2g(1+N_{s})+2\sqrt{(g-1)(1+N_{s})\big(g+(g-1)N_{s}\big)}\right]\left[-1+2g(1+N_{s})+2\sqrt{g(1+N_{s})(g-1+gN_{s})}\right]}{\left[g+(g-1)(2N_{s}+1)+\sqrt{(g-1)(N_{s}+1)((g-1)N_{s}+g)}+(g-1)(N_{s}+1)\sqrt{\frac{gN_{s}+g}{gN_{s}+g-1}}\right]^{2}}\right)\,,

Moreover, thanks to Eq. (
122
), we can also obtain the coherent information quantity appearing in Theorem
45
and Theorem
48
:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptI_{c}(A\,\rangle\,B)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(g,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=h\!\left(gN_{s}+g-1\right)-h\!\left((g-1)(N_{s}+1)\right)\,,

(386)

where
h(x)\coloneqq(x+1)\log_{2}(x+1)-x\log_{2}x
. By taking the limit of infinite energy
N_{s}\rightarrow\infty
, one obtains

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\lim\limits_{N_{s}\rightarrow\infty}H_{1/2}(A|B)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(g,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{g-1}{g}\right)\,,

(387)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{N_{s}\rightarrow\infty}H_{1/2}(A|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(g,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{g}{g-1}\right)\,,

and, thanks to Eq. (
119
), one also obtains that

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\lim\limits_{N_{s}\rightarrow\infty}I_{c}(A\,\rangle\,B)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(g,N_{s})}}

\displaystyle=\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{g}{g-1}\right)\,.

(388)

By putting together Eq. (
387
), Eq. (
388
), Theorem
45
, and Theorem
48
, we obtain a lower bound on the
n
-shot capacities of the pure amplifier channel.

Theorem 68

((Lower bound on the
n
-shot capacities of the pure amplifier channel))
.

Let
g>1
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, and
n\in\mathds{N}
such that
n\geq 2\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
. The
n
-shot quantum capacity, the
n
-shot two-way quantum capacity, and the
n
-shot secret key capacity of the pure amplifier channel satisfies:

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptQ^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\Phi_{g})

\displaystyle\geq nQ(\Phi_{g})-\sqrt{n}4\log_{2}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{g-1}{g}}+\sqrt{\frac{g}{g-1}}+1\right)\sqrt{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{9}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)}+\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{3\varepsilon^{4}}{2^{18}}\right)\,,

(389)

\displaystyle K^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\Phi_{g})

\displaystyle\geq Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\Phi_{g})\geq nQ_{2}(\Phi_{g})-\sqrt{n}4\log_{2}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{g-1}{g}}+\sqrt{\frac{g}{g-1}}+1\right)\sqrt{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{8}{\varepsilon}\right)}-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{16}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)\,,

where

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptQ(\Phi_{g})

\displaystyle=Q_{2}(\Phi_{g})=\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{g}{g-1}\right)

(390)

is the quantum capacity of the pure amplifier channel, which is equal to its two-way quantum capacity.

By using (
385
), (
386
), Theorem
45
, and Theorem
48
, we can also find a lower bound on the energy-constrained
n
-shot capacities of the pure amplifier channel, as stated in the following theorem.

Theorem 69

((Lower bound on the energy-constrained
n
-shot capacities of the pure amplifier channel))
.

Let
N_{s}>0
,
g>1
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, and
n\in\mathds{N}
such that
n\geq 2\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)
. The energy-constrained
n
-shot quantum, two-way quantum, and secret-key capacity of the pure amplifier channel satisfy

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0ptQ^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\Phi_{g},N_{s})

\displaystyle\geq n\left(h\!\left(gN_{s}+g-1\right)-h\!\left((g-1)(N_{s}+1)\right)\right)

(391)

\displaystyle-\sqrt{n}4\log_{2}\left(\sqrt{2^{H_{1/2}(A|B)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(g,N_{s})}}}}+\sqrt{2^{H_{1/2}(A|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(g,N_{s})}}}}+1\right)\sqrt{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{2^{9}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)}+\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{3\varepsilon^{4}}{2^{18}}\right)\,,

\displaystyle K^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\Phi_{g},N_{s})

\displaystyle\geq Q_{2}^{(\varepsilon,n)}(\Phi_{g},N_{s})

\displaystyle\geq n\left(h\!\left(gN_{s}+g-1\right)-h\!\left((g-1)(N_{s}+1)\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\sqrt{n}4\log_{2}\left(\sqrt{2^{H_{1/2}(A|B)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(g,N_{s})}}}}+\sqrt{2^{H_{1/2}(A|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(g,N_{s})}}}}+1\right)\sqrt{\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{8}{\varepsilon}\right)}-\log_{2}\!\left(\frac{16}{\varepsilon^{2}}\right)\,\,,

where
H_{1/2}(A|B)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(g,N_{s})}}
and
H_{1/2}(A|E)_{\Psi_{ABE}^{(g,N_{s})}}
are reported in (
386
).
